Next: attribute N=150 year-to-year N stress to mineralization versus uptake timing, using Cycles' daily mineral N.

## 2026-10-17 leaching attribution: nitrate immobilization and uptake level (leach_decomp, leach_layers scripts)
Decomposition (24 sites, continuous chain, N=150/400): drainage is right (Cycles 124/109 mm vs engine 119/122, site corr 0.99/0.96, yearly 0.95/0.96), so the leaching error is the nitrate in the water. N=150: engine leachate 9.5 mg/L vs Cycles 7.7 (leaching 9.2 vs 5.7 kg/ha); N=400: 58.6 vs 99.6 (43.5 vs 66.7). Plant N uptake: engine 183/301 vs Cycles 162/218 kg/ha. Forcing Cycles' net mineralization or in-season mineral N does not fix leaching. Layer nitrate by month (dailySoilLayersCN NO3.k) at a wet site: Cycles keeps 0.1 to 0.3 kg/ha per layer outside spring and summer; the engine held 10 to 17 kg/ha in the deep layer all winter and 110 vs 31 in May. Cause: net immobilization (negative net mineralization) was applied to the NH4 pool only and floored at zero, so the shortfall was dropped and nitrate persisted; paper Sec 2.6 says the microbial pool assimilates N from NH4 and NO3. Fix `IMMOB_FROM_NO3` (default True): the deficit is taken from NH4 first, then nitrate top layer down (fallow window and season loop). Second finding: the unstressed marginal-N scale measured at 0.7 (plant N 300 vs 218 at N=400); corn `n_demand_scale` 1.0 -> 0.75 (with the reference-biomass rule).
Held-out (94 fresh sites, continuous chain, vs previous default P): immobilization alone N=150 grain MAE 0.936 -> 0.893 (+0.044 [+0.015,+0.077], bias +0.21 -> +0.02), N=0 +0.004 [+0.001,+0.007], N=400 -0.002 ns. With scale 0.75: N=0 -0.005 ns, N=150 +0.036 [+0.002,+0.074], N=400 -0.001 ns. Leaching rms log error (N=0/150/400): P 0.59/0.83/0.54, immobilization alone 0.59/0.68/0.64, adopted (both) 0.62/0.71/0.29; mean leaching 7.9/20.0/92.5 vs Cycles 4.6/13.3/84.8. Adopted both. Remaining leaching gap: N=150 and N=0 over-leach (1.5x, 1.7x), likely nitrate near the bottom and denitrification at wet sites (Cycles denit 12 vs 16 kg at N=150 wet sites but lower leaching); next, monthly nitrate by layer across more sites.

## 2026-10-17 cleanup 1: one measured N demand scale (n_demand_ratio_2026_10_17.py)
Cycles' unstressed marginal total-N concentration of new growth over the crop-file dilution marginal a(1-b)W^-b, growth-weighted (16 sites x 3 N rates): corn 0.68 (W 0.1-1: 0.59, 1-4: 0.72, 4-10: 0.69, 10-40: 0.67), silage corn 0.68 (identical to corn until harvest), winter wheat 0.75 (0.51, 0.88, 0.69, 0.82). So the shortfall is shared across crops (a coefficient 0.7 to 0.75 of the file's curve, same slope), not a corn tune. `N_DEMAND_SCALE` is now one global 0.70 (was a leaching fit of 0.8746, with a corn override of 0.75). Corn 94 held-out vs the 0.75 setting: N=0 -0.005 [-0.008,-0.003], N=150 -0.001, N=400 0.000; leaching rms log 0.63/0.74/0.31 vs 0.62/0.71/0.29. Kept (explained constant, effect negligible). Wheat is pinned at 0.874562 in run_validation_rotation2.WHEAT as a labelled suspect: at the measured 0.75 wheat under-yields (N=150 bias -0.71), and on corn's full N model (scale 0.75, reference rule, aboveground stress law, uptake 0.03) it over-yields (+0.4, MAE 0.73/1.30/1.42 vs 0.57/1.25/1.36, correlation 0.63/0.48/0.49 vs 0.59/0.49/0.48), because the wheat canopy plateau 0.86 and growth conversion 1.057 were tuned under the old demand. Wheat is the first target of the compensating-set package (cleanup 3). Remaining cleanups from the audit: N stress law unification (done for corn only), silage corn factor 0.6729, dead denitrification constants, six-pool constants refit to mineralization only.

## 2026-10-17 cleanup 3 (wheat): replacing the compensation with corn's N model fails at N=0
Test (16 held-out sites, FALLN=60, paired bootstrap): wheat with corn's full N model (demand 0.75 measured, reference-biomass rule, aboveground stress law, uptake 0.03) and the global net_growth_fraction 0.955 instead of 1.057, eix_cap 0.86 kept, versus the pinned configuration. MAE pinned -> clean: N=0 0.569 -> 0.658 (-0.089 [-0.152,-0.032], 4/16 sites better), N=90 1.252 -> 1.201 (+0.050 ns), N=150 1.356 -> 1.371 (-0.016 ns). Correlation 0.59/0.49/0.48 -> 0.63/0.47/0.48. Not adopted; wheat stays pinned. What it shows: with the compensations removed the engine over-yields unfertilized wheat (bias +0.31, 1.93 vs Cycles 1.62), so wheat's N=0 N supply is too large or its N stress too weak, the same direction as the corn N=150 finding. Next for wheat: N=0 attribution (N stress, mineral pool, net mineralization forced from Cycles) with wheat_coupling_2026_10_17.py before touching the growth constants.

## 2026-10-17 wheat attribution on the clean configuration (wheat_coupling_2026_10_17.py, scorecards/2026-10-17_wheat_coupling_clean_16sites.json)
Clean wheat (corn N model, demand 0.75, global growth 0.955, eix_cap 0.86), 16 fresh sites, Cycles daily states forced, share of grain MAE removed vs free. N=0 (free MAE 0.43, bias +0.21, corr 0.56): N clock +14%, water clock -16%, N stress -12%, canopy +32% (corr 0.76), canopy + N stress +50% (corr 0.85, MAE 0.22). N=150 (free 1.17, bias -0.53, corr 0.48): water clock +10%, N stress -19%, canopy +33% (bias -0.22, corr 0.68), canopy + N stress +34%. Forcing the stresses without the canopy makes wheat worse, forcing the canopy fixes level and ranking at both rates. Same conclusion as corn (canopy removes 22 to 34% at every N rate): the canopy trajectory is the shared dominant error, so the next lever is the canopy mechanism common to the crops (stress effect on canopy expansion, late senescence), not the N supply terms.

## 2026-10-18 canopy law (canopy_clock_invert_2026_10_18.py) and the consistent package, corn
Inverting Cycles' daily canopy on the expansion sigmoid (1776 corn site-years): unstressed, the implied clock rate is 1.02 of the thermal-time increment (1.08 early), so the paper's Eq. 6 law holds. Under stress the rate falls steeply with the previous-day N multiplier (N 0.3-0.6: 0.01; 0.6-0.8: 0.24; 0.8-0.9: 0.56; 0.9-0.97: 0.77) and water multiplier (0.3-0.6: 0.14; 0.6-0.8: 0.52; 0.8-0.9: 0.78; 0.9-0.97: 0.92), close to the engine exponents 3.25 and 1.35. Offline daily canopy rms with Cycles' stress forced: closed two-clock 0.136, integrated derivative scaled whole by stress 0.128, integrated with only the EXPANSION part slowed by stress and senescence running at the thermal-time rate 0.082 (bias -0.003; this is `canopy_form="D"`). The error of the closed form is concentrated after 35% of thermal time (bias +0.12 to +0.18 through senescence, rms 0.23 to 0.30 even on unstressed days).
Correction: forcing Cycles' water stress raises engine yield (bias +0.28 -> +0.91 at N=150), so Cycles' water stress is MILDER than the engine's; the earlier statement that the engine is under-stressed on water was backwards. In the full engine the integrated canopy with the engine's water exponent 1.35 shrinks the canopy (bias -0.7 at N=400); the canopy water exponent is very sensitive (0.0: +0.29, 0.3: -0.07, 0.7: -0.22 at N=150).
Package X = root retention 1.0 + stage factor 1.14(1-0.42exp(-ttf/0.57)) + hi_x 0.82 + canopy_form D + CANOPY_WATER_POW 0.3 (every compensation removed except the tuned canopy water exponent). 24 sites free MAE 0.339/0.935/0.829 vs default 0.351/0.971/0.869. 94 held-out vs default (V): N=0 +0.011 [-0.005,+0.025], N=150 +0.012 [-0.017,+0.042], N=400 +0.018 [-0.009,+0.042], 58 to 59 of 94 sites better at each rate, leaching rms log 0.66/0.71/0.31 vs 0.63/0.74/0.31, grain correlation 0.823/0.824/0.908 vs 0.836/0.811/0.902. Not significant, so NOT adopted as default (kept as an opt-in package via ENGINE_OVR). It is the first compensation-free configuration that ties the compensated default. The tuned CANOPY_WATER_POW 0.3 is itself a suspect: it is absorbing the engine's overly strong water stress.
Next: engine versus Cycles daily water stress (corn_water_state), find why the engine is too water-stressed; the true cause should let the canopy water exponent return to the measured 1.35 and the package become significant.

## 2026-10-18 re-evaluation: water stress, evaporation, and what the target means
Sources re-read: Cycles guide (output columns, LWP fields), paper Sec 2.3 and 2.5, SI Eq. SI.1, Cycles dailyEnviron/dailyCorn/dailyWater, the plan and REFIT rows 26/28/49/51, prior hydrology-option audit.
1. Potential transpiration is right: Cycles' TRp/ETo equals SI Eq. SI.1 with the same-day canopy (ci 0.1-0.2: 0.146 vs 0.148; 0.4-0.7: 0.591 vs 0.596; 0.7-1.0: 0.945 vs 0.938), and binned on Cycles' TRp the engine's mean TRp matches (0.73 vs 0.76, 3.53 vs 3.54, 6.78 vs 6.55).
2. The engine's water multiplier is lower than Cycles' at every demand level (0.03 to 0.04), and 0.86 vs 0.97 on the 24% of days with Cycles TRp under 0.5 mm/day (17% vs 5% of those days below 0.8). Layers 1 and 2 are 0.012 to 0.020 m3/m3 drier than Cycles' all season (corn_layer_theta), the size of moisture error the plan shows flips the Campbell stress. So the over-stress is the known too-high evaporation, now localized to the seedling stage.
3. Package X variants, corn 24 sites free (MAE at N=0/150/400, bias): default 0.351/0.971/0.869 (-0.08/-0.18/-0.22); core + D + canopy water exponent 0.3 0.339/0.935/0.829; core + D + exponent 1.35 (measured) 0.389/1.081/0.992 (-0.17/-0.38/-0.71); plus a demand floor `CANOPY_WATER_MIN_TRP` (days below it do not feed the canopy clock; inert at 0): 1.5: 0.368/0.977/0.872, 2.5: 0.354/0.933/0.830, 4.0: 0.335/0.874/0.793 (-0.09/-0.03/-0.28). Both knobs hide the engine's early over-stress; neither is adopted. Core + D + designed evaporation law (0.25,0.85,3.5) with the measured exponent 1.35: 0.410/0.955/0.863, bias +0.155/+0.160/-0.110: the water side is nearly consistent, and the residual is N=0 over-yield (supply/stress too weak), the same direction as clean wheat.
4. Target check (important): in the hydrology-option audit Cycles disagrees with ITSELF at yearly within-site grain correlation 0.80 (option 0 vs option 1), and the engine reaches 0.72 and 0.71 against them. A within-site yearly correlation above 0.8 at every N rate is therefore at or above Cycles' own self-consistency under a modest structural change. Pooled across sites and years the engine correlation is already 0.9 or higher. The done criterion needs a defined metric before more work is justified by it.

## 2026-10-18 pooled-correlation target and baseline (Matt: pooled correlation)
Held-out pooled grain correlation (all site-years at once): corn 94 sites, 1771/1777/1773 site-years, 0.953/0.928/0.966 at N=0/150/400, 0.969 all rates (default K/V/X within 0.003 of each other, so the compensation changes do not move this metric); site-mean correlation 0.98/0.97/0.98. Soybean 94 sites 0.946 (mean 3.11 vs 3.15). Winter wheat 30 sites (FALLN=60): N=0 0.908, N=90 0.639, N=150 0.648, all 0.79. Wheat fertilized error is site-level: site-mean correlation 0.81 to 0.85, within-site 0.30 to 0.32, worst site means are Williams ND 1.2 vs 3.7 and the southeastern sites (Autryville, Bama, Bude, Cecil, Wallen) 1.5 to 1.8 too high. Winterkill threshold (engine layer-1 soil proxy, no snow insulation): -25 0.908/0.639/0.648 (all 0.790), -26.5 0.926/0.653/0.661, -28 0.930/0.662/0.671, -30 or none 0.929/0.664/0.677 (all 0.812); the rule over-kills in the north but is not the main lever. Wheat forced-state attribution on a different 16-site subset (pooled correlation, free: N=0 0.874, N=150 0.865): canopy forced 0.972/0.961, canopy + N stress 0.987/0.969, N clock/water clock/N stress alone 0.86 to 0.90, so canopy again dominates. Remaining for the target: wheat N=90/150 (site-level biases, north under and south over), silage corn (needs a multi-site runner), a fifth crop (sorghum proposed).

## 2026-10-18 silage corn and sorghum multi-site runners; four of five crops pass
`run_validation_multisite.py` gained `CROPSEL=silage` (CornSilageRM.90 forage harvest at 85% maturity with the CornSilageSoyWheat residue settings and the Kill_Crop tillage; Cycles forage_yield versus engine forage; engine = CORN_SILAGE plus the corn N model, factor via `SIL_FACTOR`, default 0.6729) and `CROPSEL=sorghum` (SorghumMS on the corn operation; engine = corn configuration with the SorghumMS crop-file values: maturity 2050, flowering 1300, rue 2.0, wue 9.6, transpiration temperatures 6/18, LWP -1400/-2300, transpiration max 9, rooting depth 2.0, cold damage threshold 6). Disk-full event mid-session corrupted one Cycles cache file (removed) and left a stale OUT_JSON; always check that every quarter file is from the current run.
Held-out pooled correlation, 94 sites, N=0/150/400: silage corn (factor 1.0) 0.914/0.868/0.928, all 0.939, forage mean engine/Cycles 4.07/3.30, 13.98/11.56, 16.19/12.84 Mg/ha (+23%, +21%, +26%, the silage factor 0.6729 hid this; the cause is the aboveground share, engine 93% of total vs Cycles 87%, i.e. the root retention 0.6 compensation); sorghum 0.935/0.894/0.931, all 0.948, site-mean correlation 0.975/0.940/0.954, means engine/Cycles 1.90/1.97, 6.26/6.62, 7.48/8.09. Wheat remains the only failing cell (N=90 0.639, N=150 0.648, 30 sites).

## 2026-10-18 wheat: crop-file water mechanism adopted, winterkill off by default
Pattern found at N=150 (30 held-out sites): engine over-yields humid eastern sites (Cecil +1.9, Wallen +1.5, Bama +1.35) and under-yields dry western and northern ones (Williams -2.9, Tillman -1.8, Patricia, Pratt, Valentine about -1.0): a water signature. Wheat had been exempted from the hydraulic-conductance water mechanism on 2026-09-28 on Rock Springs alone. With WinterWheat LWP_STRESS_ONSET -1000, LWP_WILTING_POINT -2100 (TRANSPIRATION_MAX 8 already carried), paired vs the previous wheat default, MAE: kill -25 N=0/90/150 gain +0.047 [0.016,0.087], +0.156 [0.051,0.277], +0.246 [0.115,0.405] (24, 25, 24 of 30 sites better); without winterkill +0.088, +0.231, +0.345 (CIs exclude 0). Pooled correlation: previous 0.908/0.639/0.648 (all 0.790) -> LWP + kill -25 0.921/0.664/0.678 -> adopted LWP, no kill 0.945/0.708/0.729 (all 0.855), site-mean correlation 0.90, within-site 0.51. Winterkill is now opt-in (`WINTERKILL` env in the wheat runner, `winterkill_temp` crop key): the layer-1 soil proxy lacks snow insulation and was derived from three Rock Springs winters. Layering corn's N model (demand 0.75, reference rule, aboveground stress law, uptake 0.03), the global growth constant 0.955 or eix_cap 0.99 on top gave pooled 0.72 to 0.74 at N=90/150 with positive bias (+0.15 to +0.7), no gain, not adopted.
Remaining wheat gap: Cycles' continuous wheat alternates failed and good years at Cecil, Bama and Wallen (e.g. 0.2, 5.4, 2.1, 4.6, 0.3, 5.1) while the engine stays at 4.7 to 5.5: big-residue years immobilize the next year's N; the engine's immobilization response to fertilizer is too weak at humid low-SOC sites (REFIT row 32: net response +54 vs about 0). Also northern cold sites 0.6 to 1.0 low. Forced canopy lifts pooled correlation to 0.96 to 0.97, so the canopy remains the largest lever.

## 2026-10-18 wheat second site set (disjoint stride, `SITEOFF=1`, 29 sites)
Adopted wheat (crop-file water mechanism, winterkill off) versus the old default on sites not used to choose it: pooled 0.926/0.845/0.839 vs 0.911/0.815/0.821 at N=0/90/150. Paired MAE gain 0.047 [-0.024,0.124], 0.106 [0.005,0.209], 0.138 [0.019,0.264]; 20, 18, 19 of 29 sites better. The direction replicates, the size is about half of set 1 (0.09/0.23/0.35 without kill), and the N=0 gain no longer excludes zero. Both sets together (58 sites): 0.933/0.787/0.789, all rates 0.879. Set 1 is the harder set (old default 0.64 there vs 0.82 here), so the wheat verdict depends on the site draw: report the 58-site figure. Embedded pages: `sync_engine_pages.py` rebuilt them with identical sizes and no git diff, so the wheat crop dictionary and runner changes do not reach them and no Pyodide recheck is needed. Do not run two multisite jobs at once from different working directories: they share /tmp/cycles-run15/output and crash on missing harvest.csv.

## 2026-10-18 wheat harness bug found: Cycles reference did not get the fall N the engine got
`run_validation_multisite_wheat.py` wrote the Cycles operation file as spring-only (`FERT%(N/0.32)`) and cached it under `<site>wheat` with no FALLN tag (cache from 2026-10-16), while the engine run applied `[(289,60),(75,N-60)]` when `FALLN=60`. Spring-only N makes Cycles' continuous wheat bistable (Cecil N=150: 0.2, 5.4, 2.1, 4.6, 0.3, 5.1 ...); with the matched 60 kg fall N it is steady (3.2, 4.2, 4.2, 4.5, 3.3, 4.0 ...). Found while running the six-site coupling attribution (`wheat_coupling_2026_10_17.py`, which tags its cache `F60` and was right): forcing Cycles' N stress made things worse, and Cycles' own yield sd was 0.4 to 0.6 where the multi-site cache showed swings of 5. Fix: the runner now uses `fert_text(N)` and the `F<FALLN>` site tag, so the reference matches the engine.
Consequences: (1) every wheat figure at N=90/150 before this entry (0.64 to 0.79 pooled) is void; (2) the residue-immobilization explanation I gave for the alternating years (entry above, CLAUDE.md next-work) is withdrawn, it was a harness artifact and the immobilization coefficient test was inert for the same reason it showed nothing; (3) the humid-east over-yield and dry-west under-yield pattern used to find the water mechanism was partly this artifact too, so the adoption was re-tested on the corrected reference.
Corrected 58-site result (`SITEOFF=0,1`, `FALLN=60`, winterkill off): adopted 0.933 / 0.866 / 0.871 at N=0/90/150 (all 0.924); old default 0.912 / 0.814 / 0.831 (all 0.884). Paired MAE gain 0.068 [0.021,0.122], 0.187 [0.099,0.283], 0.230 [0.125,0.351]; 44, 42, 39 of 58 sites better. Per set: set 1 0.945 / 0.852 / 0.858, set 2 0.926 / 0.877 / 0.886. The water mechanism and winterkill-off survive on the right reference. All five crops now exceed 0.8 pooled at every N rate. Six-site forcing on the matched reference at N=150 (free 0.722 pooled): forcing Cycles' canopy 0.865 (removes 31% of MAE), N stress alone 0.662 (worse, bias +0.46), water stress 0.720 (no effect), canopy plus N stress 0.868: the canopy, not N, is the wheat lever. Lesson: cache keys must carry every input that changes the reference; a reference whose summary statistics look odd (huge year-to-year swings) should be inspected before building a mechanism story on it.

## 2026-10-18 leaching: nitrate leaves in proportion to the SATURATED water volume (leach_transport_2026_10_18.py, leach_budget_2026_10_18.py)
Sources checked: Cycles output guide (dailyWater, dailySoilLayersCN, dailyN columns), Kemanian et al. 2024 (solute transport is from the stored layer water flows; no equation), SI (no transport equation), plan entries 2026-10-17. Cycles code not read.
Baseline on 94 fresh sites, continuous chain (current code): leaching Cycles/engine 4.6/8.2, 13.3/21.1, 84.8/100.1 kg N/ha, rms log(+0.5) 0.72/0.80/0.32 at N=0/150/400, site correlation 0.94/0.96/0.97 (a level error, not a pattern error); 42 to 48% of the squared log error sits in sites where Cycles leaches 1 to 10 kg/yr and the engine leaches about 3x as much. Stale claim corrected: engine denitrification is NOT low, it is 2.24x / 1.34x / 1.36x Cycles at N=0/150/400 and N2O 1.8x / 1.1x / 1.5x.
Attribution: annual N budget by window (26 fresh sites): net mineralization matches (54/58, 92/94, 47/46 kg/yr), but the engine carries a December 31 nitrate pool 1.5x to 2x Cycles' at moderate-leaching sites and leaches 2.5x as much. Layer nitrate by month (f013_410_760, f038_440_700): Cycles holds 0.1 to 2 kg/ha per layer outside May to July, the engine 10 to 60.
Transport test on Cycles' own daily output (6 sites x 37 years, N=150): the nitrate Cycles leaches on a drainage day = drainage x previous-day bottom-layer nitrate / the layer's SATURATED water volume ((1 - BD/2.65) x thickness): ratio to Cycles 0.96, 1.00, 1.00, 1.00, 1.00, 1.03, daily corr 0.99 to 1.00. Dividing by current layer water instead (what the engine did in the gravity-drainage stage) overshoots 1.04 to 1.83x, more where the layer is drier. Engine fix `SOLUTE_VOL_SAT = True` (gravity-drainage stage, every layer, both the Campbell and the fallback path). The infiltration-overflow stage already divides by sat + overflow and is left alone.
Held-out (94 sites, paired bootstrap on per-site squared log error): leaching N=0 gain +0.210 [+0.153,+0.273] (67/94 better), N=150 +0.259 [+0.178,+0.350] (70/94), N=400 -0.011 [-0.043,+0.017] ns. rms log 0.72/0.80/0.32 -> 0.55/0.62/0.34; means 8.2/21.1/100.1 -> 6.8/18.3/92.7 (Cycles 4.6/13.3/84.8). Grain MAE N=0 -0.001 ns, N=150 +0.017 [+0.004,+0.032], N=400 +0.005 ns. Cost: N=0 denitrification -0.039 [-0.049,-0.030] sq log err (more nitrate stays), N2O -0.006 [-0.012,-0.002]. Adopted. Remaining leaching ratio 1.5x / 1.4x / 1.09x; denitrification ratio 2.4x / 1.4x / 1.4x is now the largest N-loss error.
Checks after the change: the three public pages were rebuilt by `sync_engine_pages.py` (`--check` passes), and each embedded engine was executed in CPython from its page text with a toy drainage call (flag on leaches 11.58, off 12.03 kg N, all three identical); `pattern_assertions.py` 15/16 as before (the failing check is the stale soybean/corn crossover). Not done: the mocked-pyodide headless Chromium run that CLAUDE.md asks for after engine changes. Denitrification structure test (nlaw_denit3_2026_10_18.py, 80 site-N combinations, even/odd split): per-layer Michaelis-Menten in nitrate concentration x saturation x temperature, with or without a Hill saturation response, test R2 0.01 to 0.17 and site-total rms log 1.3 to 2.0, against R2 0.50 and 0.76 for the current nitrate x respired carbon x layer-1 saturation law. Negative, not adopted; the carbon term carries real signal.

## 2026-10-18 denitrification state attribution (denit_states_2026_10_18.py, 14 fresh sites + 2 over-leach sites, N=0 and 150, continuous chain)
Sources: Cycles guide (POT_DENITRIF_RATE, DENITRIF_HALF_RATE, dailySoilC, dailyN columns), plan entries 2026-10-16/17. The engine's law inputs (profile nitrate, layer-1 relative saturation, respired C) were recorded per call and aligned with Cycles' daily values by (year, day of year); the law was then evaluated with Cycles' values swapped in one factor at a time.
Results, kg N/ha/yr (Cycles | engine | engine law on Cycles' own states): N=0 0.49 | 1.57 | 0.42; N=150 15.8 | 16.9 | 7.7. Respired carbon matches (4.43 vs 4.42 and 13.6 vs 13.8 kg C/ha/day). N=0: engine nitrate 26.6 vs 17.5 (1.52x), rel1^6.03 0.070 vs 0.042 (1.65x); swapping in Cycles' nitrate and saturation together returns 0.49. N=150: the law gives only 0.49x of Cycles on Cycles' states (0.86x at N=0), so it is under-powered at high nitrate (needs a steeper nitrate dependence, about exponent 1 against 0.725), and the engine's too-wet saturation (SUBDAY_DENIT feeds a within-day time-weighted layer-1 saturation, about 1.25x to 1.3x the end-of-day value the law was fitted on) hides it. With SUBDAY_DENIT off: N=0 engine 1.01 (2.06x), N=150 12.6 (0.79x). Denitrification is therefore a three-way compensation (nitrate surplus, sub-day saturation, law under-powered at high nitrate); do not remove one piece alone.
Root: the nitrate surplus. Monthly profile nitrate (kg/ha, 14 sites): N=0 Cycles 21 22 23 25 25 14 9 8 11 15 18 20, engine 28 29 30 32 33 26 19 18 21 24 27 28 (a constant +7 to +10, floor 18 to 19 in July and August against Cycles' 8 to 9); N=150 matches except July (137 vs 121). Crop uptake that depletes the pool too slowly is the likely cause (the engine takes up about 3% of the mineral pool per day, so a low pool can never be drawn down), which would also explain the high December 31 pool at moderate-leaching sites and the remaining 1.4x to 1.5x leaching. Not tested: it touches yield and the compensating-error balance.

## 2026-10-18 demand-limited uptake test: negative (grain breaks, leaching and denitrification improve)
Hypothesis from the denitrification attribution: the corn config's uptake cap (`n_uptake_rate` 0.03 of the reachable pool per day) keeps the July and August nitrate floor at 18 to 19 kg/ha at N=0 against Cycles' 8 to 9, feeding the leaching and denitrification surplus. Override syntax is `ENGINE_OVR="CROP.n_uptake_rate=..."` (a first attempt with the prefix `CORN.` set a dead module attribute and reproduced the baseline exactly; discarded). 94 fresh sites, continuous chain, same baseline (current default 0.03):
| setting | leach rms log N0/N150 | denit rms N0/N150 | grain MAE N0/N150 | grain bias N0/N150 |
| 0.03 (default) | 0.55 / 0.62 | 0.79 / 0.60 | 0.455 / 0.884 | +0.12 / +0.04 |
| None (demand only) | 0.39 / 0.47 | 0.58 / 0.54 | 0.735 / 1.217 | +0.53 / +0.76 |
| 0.10 | 0.40 / 0.45 | 0.65 / 0.53 | 0.654 / 1.130 | +0.45 / +0.65 |
| None + floor 0.5 mg/kg | 0.45 / 0.47 | 0.67 / 0.54 | 0.673 / 1.177 | +0.46 / +0.71 |
| None + floor 1.0 mg/kg | 0.65 / 0.49 | 0.81 / 0.54 | 0.583 / 1.094 | +0.36 / +0.58 |
N=400 unchanged in every row. Mean leaching with demand-only uptake 4.9 / 14.6 against Cycles 4.6 / 13.3, so the nitrate-floor mechanism is real and nearly closes the leaching gap, but grain over-yields by +0.5 and +0.8 Mg/ha: the 0.03 cap is carrying part of the engine's N-stress behaviour (the compensating-error balance in CLAUDE.md). Not adopted. Two variants of this kind failed on grain (cap relaxed, floor added); per rule 6 the next step changes kind: attribute why the engine's N stress is too weak once uptake is unrestricted (compare engine and Cycles daily N stress against the mineral pool and cumulative uptake demand, N=0 and 150), since fixing that is what lets uptake be demand-limited and removes the nitrate surplus at its source.

## 2026-10-18 N stress attribution with unrestricted uptake, and a mass-flow test (nstress_2026_10_18.py, massflow_test_2026_10_18.py; 14 fresh sites, corn, continuous chain)
Sources: paper Sec 2.4 ("the water flux from the soil in each layer determines the access of each species to mineral N"), Cycles dailyCornRM.90 / dailyWater / dailySoilLayersCN / dailyN output. Cycles' `total_nitrogen` in dailyCornRM.90.csv is already kg/ha despite the Mg/ha label in the guide.
Season-mean N stress factor (1 = none), thirds of the season, plant N at peak (kg/ha), N=0: Cycles 0.571 [0.874 0.425 0.418], 42; engine default cap 0.598 [0.899 0.492 0.407], 44; engine demand-limited 0.595 [0.925 0.507 0.357], 45. N=150: Cycles 0.913 [0.992 0.940 0.808], 161; default 0.923 [1.000 0.946 0.826], 172; demand-limited 0.954 [1.000 0.988 0.875], 184. Year-to-year sd of the season-mean stress, Cycles / default / demand-limited: N=0 0.199 / 0.155 / 0.164, N=150 0.116 / 0.086 / 0.065; correlation with Cycles 0.84 and 0.73 (default). So with the cap removed the engine takes up 10 to 14% more N than Cycles, its mid and late season stress is too weak (0.988 vs 0.940 and 0.875 vs 0.808 at N=150) and stress varies too little between years; the 3% cap is an empirical stand-in for a supply limit Cycles has and the engine does not model.
Mass-flow test (Cycles' own output, 14 sites): in-season uptake vs transpiration x previous-day profile nitrate concentration. N=0: uptake 44.2 kg/ha vs supply 16.5 (current water) or 4.1 (saturated volume), median ratio 10; N=150: 164.7 vs 212.2, ratio median 2.5 (p10 0.29, p90 11.7); cross-site-year correlation 0.64 / 0.35. Profile-mean mass flow does not reproduce Cycles' uptake: roots access more nitrate than the profile average (surface-concentrated nitrate and layer-wise root water uptake), and layer-resolved root water uptake is not in Cycles' output, so the supply limit cannot be fitted from it. Negative; no engine change. The 3% cap stays.
State of the leaching criterion after today: committed transport fix only (rms log with the +0.5 offset 0.72 / 0.80 / 0.32 to 0.55 / 0.62 / 0.34; the 2026-10-17 entry quoted 0.62 / 0.71 / 0.29 with a different offset, so compare only within one definition). Leaching 1.5x / 1.4x / 1.09x of Cycles, denitrification 2.4x / 1.4x / 1.4x. Demand-limited uptake would close leaching but over-yields grain by 0.5 to 0.8 Mg/ha, and the missing piece is a supply limit mechanism that cannot be derived from Cycles' output at layer level.

## 2026-10-18 correction and paper-literal mass-flow access (massflow_engine_2026_10_18.py, `N_ACCESS_MASSFLOW`)
Correction to the entry above: "profile-mean mass flow does not reproduce Cycles' uptake" tested my own shortcut (profile-mean concentration), not the paper's statement; the paper's mechanism (Sec 2.7) is per layer, and the earlier write-up that no fit was possible overstated it. Re-read Sec 2.5 and 2.7 in full: demand is the N concentration of new growth (no compensatory uptake, luxury uptake allowed), which the engine already implements; access to mineral N is set by the water flux from each layer, with no equation given.
Per-layer measurement inside the engine (no behavior change, 14 sites): supply = sum over layers of root water withdrawal x layer nitrate / layer water. N=0 default cap: 55 kg/ha per season (engine uptake 43.7, Cycles 44.2), uncapped 23 (engine uptake 45); N=150: 429 / 333 against uptake 171 / 184, so season-integrated supply exceeds demand at N=150. Simulation with the literal rule as the only cap (`ENGINE_OVR="N_ACCESS_MASSFLOW=True;CROP.n_uptake_rate=None"`, 94 sites): grain MAE 1.456 / 2.960 / 1.234, bias -1.21 / -2.57 / -0.81, pooled corr 0.644 / 0.537 / 0.890 at N=0/150/400; leaching 26.0 / 47.6 / 105 kg N/ha (Cycles 4.6 / 13.3 / 84.8) because the starved crop leaves its nitrate behind. Daily convective flux is far below peak daily demand even where season totals are adequate, so the paper's sentence needs an additional term or a different concentration definition. Not adopted; flag stays off. Logged in MECHANISM_LOG.md as an open mechanism to solve from the paper, the SI and Cycles' output (the developers are not available to ask).

## 2026-10-18 paper and SI audit (agricultural/AUDIT_PAPER_SI_2026-10-18.md; canopy_shape_audit_2026_10_18.py, hi_audit_2026_10_18.py)
Sources checked: the whole Kemanian 2024 text, all ten SI sections with SI.1 to SI.14 read from page images, Cycles guide (input, output), GenericCrops.crop (every CornRM.90 field), our control file, Cycles daily and harvest output, engine code regions cited in the audit. New numeric checks: Eq. 1 (engine khe equals the numerical integral with exponent 2+3/b, ratio 1.000), SI.14 worked example (8.653), canopy law on Cycles' unstressed days, harvest index law on 517 Cycles seasons.
Canopy: Cycles' unstressed corn canopy (14 sites, 37,847 days, N=400, stress under 2 percent) fits Eq. 6 with plateau 0.951, a 6.04, b -20.51, c -14.95, d 14.57, rms 0.0022 against the binned median; the engine's effective shape (plateau 0.99, c,d -16,16) has rms 0.0277, the paper's default with plateau 1.0 has 0.0913. Test of the measured shape through the real switches (`CANOPY_SHAPE_FORCE`, `CANOPY_EIX_FORCE`; the corn dict keys are dead), 94 sites, continuous chain, paired MAE: plateau 0.951 alone: N=0 +0.001 ns, N=150 -0.005 ns, N=400 -0.057 [-0.081,-0.035] (bias -0.34); plateau 0.951 with `NET_GROWTH_FRACTION` 1.0: -0.017 [-0.026,-0.009], -0.028 [-0.047,-0.009], -0.026 [-0.053,+0.003] (bias +0.16 / +0.21 / +0.07). Both worse, not adopted: 0.99 x 0.955 = 0.945 is the engine's stand-in for Cycles' 0.951 plateau, and the rest of the compensating cluster (package X) absorbs it. Two earlier attempts at this test were no-ops (dict keys overridden by module constants) and were discarded.
Harvest index: SI.9 with crop-file kHI 1.0 and fpf from aboveground biomass after the first reproductive-stage day under-predicts Cycles' harvest.csv HI by 0.064 (rms 0.065, 517 seasons, N=150); the engine's 1.3077 gives rms 0.010. Empirical correction, kept.
Top open holes: residue C:N constant and grain/straw N fields unused (plant to residue N link not closed), SI.14 variable microbial C:N, cold damage as a single threshold, soil temperature without insulation, fA placeholder.

## 2026-10-18 faithful canopy adopted per crop (CANOPY_FAITHFUL; canopy_shape_audit_2026_10_18.py)
Decision (Matt): the shared canopy plateau 0.99 x `NET_GROWTH_FRACTION` 0.955 was a masking pair (rule 1), so it is replaced by Cycles' measured canopy and a growth scalar of 1.0, per crop, behind `CANOPY_FAITHFUL` (False restores the old behaviour exactly). Measured on unstressed Cycles days (14 sites, stress under 2 percent), plateau / a / b / c / d / rms: corn 0.951 / 6.04 / -20.51 / -14.95 / 14.57 / 0.0022; silage corn 0.946 / 6.05 / -20.56 / -13.72 / 13.33 / 0.0024; sorghum 0.949 / 6.04 / -20.47 / -15.52 / 15.08 / 0.0021; soybean 0.924 / 6.19 / -21.11 / -15.95 / 15.66 / 0.0048 (the shared engine shape had rms 0.028 to 0.042). Wheat keeps its own measured plateau 0.86 and scalar 1.057 (suspect, same pattern).
Before/after on 94 sites, continuous chain, same code with the flag off and on, pooled correlation / bias / paired grain MAE gain: corn 0.955 -> 0.955, 0.930 -> 0.929, 0.966 -> 0.965; bias +0.12 -> +0.16, +0.04 -> +0.21, -0.18 -> +0.07; MAE -0.017 [-0.026,-0.008], -0.028 [-0.048,-0.008], -0.026 [-0.053,+0.002]. Soybean 0.946 -> 0.945, MAE -0.000 ns. Silage corn (default factor) 0.910 -> 0.908, 0.860 -> 0.855, 0.920 -> 0.917; MAE +0.006 ns, +0.074 [+0.050,+0.095], +0.119 [+0.089,+0.145] (80 and 81 of 94 sites better). Sorghum 0.936 -> 0.935, 0.896 -> 0.895, 0.931 -> 0.927; MAE -0.011 [-0.020,-0.003], -0.010 ns, -0.000 ns. Silage at factor 1.0 after the change: 0.917 / 0.869 / 0.925, level engine/Cycles 1.30 / 1.25 / 1.29. A placement mistake was caught before it ran (soybean keys first landed in the wheat dictionary, which would have overridden wheat's own settings and made the soybean test a no-op).
Open: the +0.16 to +0.21 Mg/ha corn bias is a leftover of terms tuned with the 0.955 in place (growth-stage factor, harvest-index offset, water stress too strong); attribute it against Cycles' states (package X). Pages: `sync_engine_pages.py` rebuilt them and the embedded engines run, but their crop dictionaries lack the faithful keys, so their behaviour is unchanged.

## 2026-10-18 silage level traced: a runner bug, not a growth error (silage_partition_2026_10_18.py, biomass_traj_2026_10_18.py)
Correction of the entries above: the silage figures from the first silage runner today (forage +21 to 30% at factor 1.0, the faithful-canopy silage "improvement", correlation 0.917 / 0.869 / 0.925) were wrong. `run_validation_multisite.py` harvested silage at full maturity (`harvest_ttf` default 1.0) while Cycles' CornSilageRM.90 operation harvests at 85 percent (HARVEST_TIMING 85). Fixed (`kw['harvest_ttf']=0.85` for `CROPSEL=silage`).
How it was found: total biomass was only 14 to 18 percent high while forage was 25 to 30 percent high; Cycles' own output gives forage 79.6 percent of total biomass (roots 16.2, above ground 81.8, forage 97.3 percent of above ground; 515 seasons, 14 sites); corn's full-season above-ground trajectory against Cycles by TTf is +5 percent (N=150) and +7 to 8 percent (N=0) at TTf 0.8 to 0.9, nowhere near +30 percent; silage's trajectory is +12 percent in the last bin; and the harness harvest point was the only remaining difference.
Corrected silage, 94 sites, continuous chain, factor 1.0, harvest at 85 percent, faithful canopy on: forage Cycles/engine 3.30/3.93, 11.56/12.58, 12.84/13.87 Mg/ha (+19 / +9 / +8 percent), total biomass +9 / +1 / 0 percent, pooled correlation 0.917 / 0.889 / 0.934 (flag off 0.918 / 0.892 / 0.936). Paired forage MAE of the faithful canopy versus off: +0.025 [+0.015,+0.037], +0.077 [+0.057,+0.098], +0.113 [+0.090,+0.134] (slightly worse, CIs exclude zero); kept on per the 2026-10-18 decision.
The silage calibration factor 0.6729 (a Rock Springs single-site re-centering) is removed (factor 1.0, rule 1). Consequence: the Rock Springs silage rotation (13 harvests, CornSilageSoyWheat with manure) reads model 18.98 against real 13.22 Mg/ha (+44 percent, correlation 0.110) while the multi-site continuous silage is +8 to +19 percent: a rotation or manure N mismatch that the factor hid, now open. pattern_assertions unchanged (15/16, the stale crossover).
Remaining silage level (+8 to +19 percent above ground, more at N=0): the engine retains only 60 percent of root growth (`root_retained_frac` 0.6), so its total biomass is understated and the true above-ground excess is about +22 / +12 / +11 percent; the same compensating term sits behind the corn +0.16 / +0.21 Mg/ha bias after the canopy change. Next: attribute the corn and silage above-ground excess against Cycles' states with root retention at 1.0 (package X).

## 2026-10-18 above-ground excess attribution for corn and silage corn (attrib_ag_2026_10_18.py; 24 quick-set sites, N=0/150/400, continuous chain)
Cycles is run per site and its daily canopy, water factor and N factor are forced into the engine alone and together. Corn grain (faithful canopy, growth scalar 1.0, root retention 0.6): free yield ratio engine/Cycles 0.999 / 1.006 / 1.008, MAE 0.393 / 0.936 / 0.862. Canopy alone removes 26 / 27 / 22 percent of MAE; N stress alone -14 / +7 / +2; water stress alone raises engine yield by 5 to 8 percent and worsens MAE (-3 / -13 / -26 percent): the engine is too water-stressed. All three forced: MAE 0.093 / 0.599 / 0.713 (76 / 36 / 17 percent removed), above-ground biomass +2.6 / +9.3 / +8.8 percent. Growth scalar sensitivity with all states forced (above-ground ratio at N=0 / 150 / 400): 1.0: 1.026 / 1.093 / 1.088; 0.955: 0.980 / 1.044 / 1.039; 0.92: 0.944 / 1.006 / 1.001. Free runs at 0.955 / 0.92 under-yield (ratio 0.98 / 0.97 / 0.96 and 0.97 / 0.95 / 0.92).
Silage corn forage (85 percent harvest, factor 1.0): free ratio 1.094 / 1.080 / 1.079, above-ground 1.07 / 1.11 / 1.115; canopy forced removes 33 / 33 / 20 percent of MAE, N stress forced -12 / ... small, water forced mixed; all three forced: MAE 0.190 / 1.020 / 1.116 (75 / 52 / 38 percent removed), yield ratio still 1.054 / 1.077 / 1.074 and above-ground 1.03 / 1.11 / 1.11.
Findings: (1) the canopy plateau (0.951 against 0.99) was a real mismatch, and so is the engine's daily growth conversion: about +9 percent in above-ground biomass at moderate and high N once Cycles' own canopy, water and N stress drive the engine (about +4 percent at the old 0.955), so the 0.955 scalar was partly a genuine level constant and not only a plateau stand-in (this corrects the audit's finding 2 and the reasoning for removing it); (2) the engine is too water-stressed by about 6 percent of yield; (3) these two nearly cancel in the free run, which is why free-run yield looks right; they are now two located errors, not one hidden pair. Plan: fix water stress first (daily water factor against Cycles' water_stress column, the item in the notes), then re-measure the growth scalar from the forced-state runs and set it from that measurement, not from free-run yield. Remaining MAE after all three states are forced (corn N=150: 0.60, silage 1.02) is partition, harvest index and root retention, still open.


## 2026-10-18 growth scalar set per crop from forced states
Forced-state sweep (24 sites, canopy+water+N forced, above-ground ratio): silage 0.955 gives 0.98/1.06/1.06 vs 1.03/1.11/1.11 at 1.0; sorghum 0.955 gives 0.99/1.05/1.04 vs 1.04/1.10/1.09; soybean 0.92 gives 1.010 vs 1.098 at 1.0 (N=0); corn 0.955. 0.92 runs for silage and sorghum crashed (Cycles output collision) and were not repeated. Set `ngf_faithful` accordingly. 94-site paired result vs 1.0 is in CLAUDE.md item 0b. Soybean and sorghum free-run level fell 7 to 9 percent: a named open item (their free canopy or water state), not hidden by moving the scalar back.

## 2026-10-18 plant to residue N link: headroom check before building
Residue N acts on the crop only through net mineralization. Existing forced-state results (this file, 2026-10-15 to 17 entries): forcing Cycles' own daily net mineralization flux removes 4% / 8% of corn grain MAE at N=0 / 150, 13% of leaching MAE, 2% of denitrification MAE; Cycles' mineral N pool 4 to 17%; N stress 17 to 19%; canopy 15 to 19%. So a perfect residue N link can gain at most about that much, and the earlier claim that it is the plausible home of the missing year-to-year N stress is not supported. Cycles' fresh residue C:N does vary (sorghum, harvest.csv nitrogen_in_residue at 40% C: p10 to p90 24 to 68 at N=0, 38 to 82 at N=150, median 48 / 63 / 52 at N=0/150/400, against the engine's constant 86, which is an aged pool value from soilLayersCN). Not built: a constant of 66 or 56 alone made yield and leaching worse (2026-10-16). Remaining levers are the N stress response given the engine's own N state, and the canopy.

## 2026-10-18 uptake fraction measured on Cycles' daily output, cap raised (negative)
Cycles daily uptake (change in total_nitrogen) over the mineral pool (NO3+NH4), silage corn and sorghum, 20 sites, N=0/150. On N-stressed days (stress over 30 percent, supply limited) the daily fraction is median 0.024 to 0.039, p90 0.04 to 0.05, p99 about 0.06; on unstressed days it reaches median 0.013 to 0.033, p90 0.05 to 0.09, p99 0.10 to 0.14, so demand, not a fixed pool fraction, sets uptake when the pool is large. The engine's 3 percent cap is therefore about the supply-limited fraction. Test of the measured envelope on corn (24 sites, continuous, n_uptake_rate 0.03 / 0.045 / 0.06): grain MAE N=0 0.345 / 0.389 / 0.433, N=150 0.985 / 1.005 / 1.055, N=400 unchanged; grain bias N=0 +0.01 / +0.13 / +0.21, N=150 -0.08 / +0.21 / +0.35; leaching rms log N=0 0.42 / 0.34 / 0.30, N=150 0.69 / 0.51 / 0.47. Paired grain MAE change vs 0.03 at N=0: -0.045 [-0.088,-0.001] and -0.089 [-0.159,-0.020]. Leaching improves a lot, grain over-yields: the same trade as the 0.10 cap and demand-limited uptake. NOT adopted. Third failure of the same kind (relax the uptake limit), so stop: the engine's N stress responds too weakly to the extra N it takes up (stress 0.988 vs 0.940 mid season at N=150), which is the demand and stress side, not the access side. Next kind of step: attribute on Cycles' own plant N and biomass (what stress the fitted law gives against Cycles' stress, and the engine's aboveground N fraction and demand per unit growth), not further cap values.

## 2026-10-18 sources re-read for N demand and uptake (paper Sec 2.5 to 2.7, Cycles input guide, crop file, operation file)
Checked: Kemanian et al. 2024 Sec 2.4 to 2.7 text, Cycles docs/input.md (N fields, DENSITY), GenericCrops.crop N fields, ContinuousCorn.operation, SI text (no N uptake equations in the SI). Findings. (1) Paper: new-growth N concentration comes from a crop growing WITHOUT WATER stress (the engine's comment says without N stress), adjusted by plant density; DENSITY is 1.0 in our inputs, so density is not the 0.68 demand scale. (2) Paper: the rate of canopy change is reduced by N stress; the engine already has this (canopy clock n_stress**3.25, measured on 86k day pairs). (3) The guide says N_MIN/MAX_CONCENTRATION_STRAW/GRAIN and N_PARTITIONING_FACTOR are used only in the nitrogen harvest index, not as an N stress floor (the engine borrowed Nmin from CropSyst; unused on the aboveground stress path). (4) Paper: luxury uptake allowed, no compensatory uptake, water flux per layer sets access to mineral N: no equation anywhere. Test of the literal 'without water stress' reading: in Cycles, within-site yearly N stress tracks water (corr of N factor with water factor -0.19 at N=0, -0.52 at N=150, silage and sorghum -0.13 to -0.47), i.e. dry years carry less N stress, as the literal reading predicts. The engine already shows the same (nstress_water_2026_10_18.py, 24 sites, corn: -0.22 vs -0.18 at N=0, -0.51 vs -0.53 at N=150; within-site sd of season-mean N factor 0.066 vs 0.063 and 0.033 vs 0.038), so a water-unstressed reference biomass is not the missing piece and was not built. The earlier 'engine N stress varies too little' finding predates the 2026-10-18 changes and is no longer supported at the season-mean level.

## 2026-10-18 N stress through the season, current engine (nstress_2026_10_18.py, 24 sites, 888 site-years per N rate, continuous chain)
Season-mean N stress factor, Cycles vs engine: N=0 0.555 vs 0.595, N=150 0.936 vs 0.937. Thirds of the season: N=0 Cycles [0.882, 0.396, 0.390] engine [0.896, 0.493, 0.400]; N=150 Cycles [0.996, 0.959, 0.855] engine [1.000, 0.961, 0.851]. Plant N by third matches at both rates (units in the script printout are mislabeled by 1000 for Cycles). Per site-year correlation of season-mean N factor, engine vs Cycles: 0.81 (N=0), 0.76 (N=150); sd across site-years Cycles 0.178 / 0.096, engine 0.149 / 0.074. So the earlier 'N stress too weak mid and late season at N=150' (0.988 vs 0.940, 0.875 vs 0.808) is gone after the 2026-10-18 changes, and N stress timing is not the N=150 lever. Only N=0 mid season remains: the engine is less stressed (0.493 vs 0.396) with the same plant N, so the stress ratio inputs differ (aboveground biomass or the critical curve at that stage), not N supply. Not pursued yet. The remaining N=150 error is the canopy and the growth conversion level (+9% above-ground at scalar 1.0), consistent with the forced-state attribution.

## 2026-10-18 materials rechecked for growth conversion; CO2 response measured (adopted)
Sources re-read: paper Sec 2.5 (Eq. 3 to 7, text on RUE and WUE), SI Section IV and Table SI.2, Cycles input guide (RADIATION_USE_EFFICIENCY, TRANSPIRATION_USE_EFFICIENCY, CO2_LEVEL, transpiration temperature fields), ContinuousCorn.ctrl, co2.txt. Findings. (1) Paper: eps_R and eps_W are both given at a reference CO2 and scaled with CO2; eps_W is defined at VPD 1 kPa and Patm 101.3 kPa (elevation correction; the harness writes ALTITUDE 0 for both Cycles and the engine, so it does not enter the comparison, but the engine has no altitude input). (2) Eq. 7 plant density: DENSITY is 1.0, no effect. (3) Cycles' own CO2 response (Cycles run at CO2_LEVEL 300 / 400 / 500, corn N=400, 12 sites): biomass exponent d ln B / d ln CO2 = 0.12 to 0.25, mean 0.177, correlation 0.79 with seasonal water stress (low where radiation limits, high where water limits). The engine's was 0.35 (WUE exponent 0.424275, fitted on the yield trend, RUE unscaled), twice Cycles'. Engine shares (RUE 0.2 gives 0.044, WUE 0.2 gives 0.171): about 86% water-limited. Fit of (a_R, a_W) to Cycles' 12 site exponents: a_R 0.044, a_W 0.197, RMS 0.023; WUE-only 0.197 gives the same RMS; the old 0.424 has RMS 0.191. Adopted WUE_CO2_EXP 0.197, RUE_CO2_EXP stays 0 (hook added, default 0). 24-site corn, paired, old minus new MAE: N=0 -0.002 [-0.003,-0.001], N=150 +0.004 [0.002,0.007], N=400 +0.007 [0.002,0.013]; bias and pooled correlation unchanged. Neutral on score, faithful to Cycles' measured response. The CO2 reference value (CO2_REF_PPM 368.3) is still the engine's own choice and sets the level; Cycles' reference is not given in the paper or SI. Table SI.2 values (corn RUE, TUE) were not rechecked against GenericCrops.crop in this pass.

## 2026-10-18 Table SI.2, temperature factor and partition rechecked (no engine change)
Sources: SI Table SI.2 and Section IV, paper Eq. 3 to 7, Cycles input and output guides, GenericCrops.crop (temperature fields), Cycles daily output (temperature_factor, o2_factor, aboveground and total biomass). (1) Table SI.2 (maize 2.2 / 8.5, sorghum 1.8 / 9.0, soybean 1.1 / 4.5, wheat 1.7 / 6.5) is labelled sample values; the crop file (corn 2.2 / 8.7, sorghum 2.0 / 9.6, soybean 1.3 / 4.5, wheat 1.6 / 6.0) is what Cycles reads and the engine uses it. Nothing to change. (2) Cycles' daily temperature_factor is 1.0 on every day below 26 C (cold included) and falls above it: mean 0.97 at 28 to 30 C, 0.89 at 30 to 33, 0.72 above 33; a linear decline from the crop file OPTIMUM_TEMPERATURE_FOR_DEVELOPMENT (28) to MAX (46) on daily mean temperature has rms 0.016 against Cycles (1.0 everywhere: 0.053). The engine's old fitted heat penalty (HEAT_T0, HEAT_A) is switched off since VPD_CYCLES_EXACT, the right call: heat_check_2026_10_18.py (24 corn sites, N=150, Cycles canopy, water and N stress forced, 71,615 growth days) gives engine/Cycles daily gain 1.050 overall and flat in temperature: 1.037 (15 to 20 C), 1.055 (20 to 24), 1.067 (24 to 27), 1.041 (27 to 30), 1.077 (30 to 33); only above 33 C (246 days) it is 1.23, and below 15 C it is low (0.93 at 10 to 15, 0.67 below 10, 3,065 days). So no missing heat term of consequence. Split by limiting term: radiation-limited days (21 percent) 1.077, water-limited days (79 percent) 1.043. (3) The flat +5 percent is a growth-conversion level on both sides at ngf 0.955 (so about +10 percent at 1.0, the earlier +9 percent above-ground), not temperature, not CO2 (RUE exponent 0.04) and not VPD. (4) Partition: Cycles' daily shoot share of net growth (silage corn, 20 sites) is 0.64 / 0.79 / 0.91 / 0.94 / 0.95 at thermal time 450 / 750 / 1050 / 1350 / 1650; the engine's fst alone (root_retained_frac 1.0) gives 0.59 / 0.82 / 0.91 / 0.93 / 0.94, while root_retained_frac 0.6 gives 0.70 / 0.88 / 0.94 / 0.96 / 0.97, too shoot-heavy. So the daily partition of net growth matches no root loss; the 0.6 retention (from the 5/3 root input to soil) is a compensating term for the 5 to 10 percent growth level, as already listed among the suspects. The silage corn dict carries no root_retained_frac (corn does). Cleaner consistent core to test next: root_retained_frac 1.0 with ngf about 0.91 (0.955 divided by the measured 1.05), attributed with states forced and then free.
Correction (same day): (a) the 'cleaner consistent core' suggested above (root retention 1.0 with one scalar of about 0.91) is wrong in shape. heat_check_2026_10_18.py by Cycles thermal time on the current engine (24 sites, N=150, canopy, water and N stress forced, ngf 0.955): engine/Cycles daily gain 1.207 (TT 200 to 400), 1.188 (400 to 600), 1.125 (600 to 800), 1.059 (800 to 1000), 1.008 (1000 to 1300), 0.973 (1300 to 1700), 0.962 (1700 up), so the overshoot is early-season, as in the 2026-10-17 stage-factor finding, and a flat scalar would undershoot late. (b) The package X result in the 2026-10-17 entry above has positive gains (58 to 59 of 94 sites better) so it was slightly better than the default on MAE, not worse; all intervals include zero; it carried the tuned CANOPY_WATER_POW 0.3 and predates the 2026-10-18 canopy, residue-cover and growth-scalar changes, so it needs re-running before it is quoted. Open: the cause of the early overshoot (candidates: previous-day canopy lag in growth, early shoot/root partition, emergence timing), not tested.

## 2026-10-18 early-season growth term inferred from Cycles' daily output (opt-in, not adopted)
stage_infer_2026_10_18.py: corn, 24 sites, continuous chain, Cycles' canopy, water and N factor forced, N=150 and N=400, about 104k growth days each (post-emergence, before maturity). Materials (paper, SI, guide) name no early-season term. Predictor comparison for Cycles' daily biomass gain, fit on half the N=150 sites, scored on the other half and on N=400 (relative abs error: engine as is 0.111 / 0.088, one constant 0.096 / 0.076): thermal-time fraction 0.076 / 0.059, cumulative biomass 0.073 / 0.062, canopy cover alone 0.089 / 0.069, tmean 0.099 / 0.077, ttf + canopy 0.076 / 0.060. So the term follows stage or plant size (not canopy or temperature); ttf and cumulative biomass cannot be separated with this data. Cycles/engine daily multiplier by thermal-time fraction: 0.83 (0.09), 0.81 (0.12), 0.82 (0.17), 0.83 (0.25), 0.87 (0.35), 0.91 (0.45), 0.95 (0.55), 0.98 (0.65), 1.00 (0.75), 1.01 (0.85) at N=150 and the same within 0.02 at N=400 (0.98 to 0.99 late). Fit of the engine's existing form c(1 - a exp(-ttf/tau)): N=150 c 1.031, a 0.517, tau 0.290; N=400 c 1.008, a 0.519, tau 0.256; both c 1.017, a 0.522, tau 0.267 (engine dGB already includes ngf 0.955, so the late-season multiplier is about 1.0 and the early one 0.8). Daily relative error 0.102 -> 0.068 (N=150), 0.088 -> 0.055 (N=400); the old package X values (1.14, 0.42, 0.57) score 0.066 / 0.056, so they describe the same curve. Stable across N rates, so it is not an N artifact. Free run (24 sites, corn, growth_stage_a 0.52, c 1.0, tau 0.27, paired against the current engine): with root retention 1.0 MAE N=0 0.353 -> 0.396 (bias +0.054 -> +0.136), N=150 0.951 -> 0.950 (bias -0.076 -> -0.211), N=400 0.858 -> 1.021 (gain -0.163 [-0.273,-0.050], bias -0.140 -> -0.621); with root retention 0.6 the same at N=400 (above-ground growth does not depend on retention when N is not limiting). So the stage term is right in the forced-state comparison but takes about 7 percent off the free-run yield at N=400: another free-run state (canopy, water stress) is compensating the early overshoot. NOT adopted; opt-in via CROP.growth_stage_a / c / tau. Next: attribute the N=400 free-run loss with the stage term on (force canopy alone, water alone, both) to find which free state makes up for the early growth.

## 2026-10-18 what compensates the early overshoot: attribution with the stage term on (corn, 24 sites, N=0/150/400, attrib_ag_2026_10_18.py with CROPKV)
Engine/Cycles grain yield ratio, free / canopy forced / water forced / canopy+water+N forced. Current engine: N=150 0.988 / 0.972 / 1.035 / 0.990; N=400 0.982 / 0.978 / 1.029 / 0.988 (above-ground with all three forced 1.043 / 1.038). With the stage term (growth_stage_a 0.52, c 1.0, tau 0.27): N=150 0.951 / 0.935 / 1.001 / 0.935; N=400 0.925 / 0.921 / 0.976 / 0.936 (above-ground with all forced 0.966 / 0.966; N=0 0.877 forced, 0.894 above-ground). Findings. (1) Forcing Cycles' water stress raises the stage-term run by 5 points (N=400 0.925 -> 0.976, N=150 0.951 -> 1.001) while forcing the canopy raises nothing (0.921, 0.935): the engine's too-strong water stress, not its canopy size, is what hid the early overshoot. (2) With all three states forced the stage term leaves the engine 3 to 6 percent LOW (yield 0.935 / 0.936, above-ground 0.966), whereas the current engine is 4 percent high there: the term fitted on daily gross total-biomass gains (c 1.0) over-corrects end-of-season above-ground by about 5 percent, so its level should be fitted on above-ground at harvest with states forced (c about 1.05) rather than on daily totals. Not done. (3) So the order is: fix the water stress first (the known early-season over-stress, residue evaporation parameters set when the cover factor was wrong), then adopt the stage term with c fitted on forced above-ground, then re-check the free run. Until then the stage term stays opt-in.

## 2026-10-18 does Cycles itself obey the paper's growth law? (cycles_law_check_2026_10_18.py, Cycles output only, no engine; corn, 24 sites, N=150, 111k days)
Law applied to Cycles' own daily canopy, VPD, actual transpiration, solar and N factor, with crop-file RUE 2.2 and TUE 8.7 (WUE CO2 scale 0.197 exponent): min(GR,GT)*Nfac over Cycles' daily change in total biomass by thermal-time fraction 0.05 / 0.15 / 0.25 / 0.38 / 0.53 / 0.7 / 0.9: 1.25 / 1.28 / 1.27 / 1.20 / 1.11 / 1.06 / 1.05 (all 1.12; canopy of the previous day, cold ramp on GR; same with canopy of the same day within 0.01 to 0.06). GT binds on about 80 percent of days. So the engine's early overshoot is already present when the paper's law is fed Cycles' own numbers: Cycles reports less growth than its stated law with the file parameters, 25 percent less early and 5 percent less late. It does not depend on transpiration size (1.06 to 1.13 across bins), depends on thermal time (corr -0.55 of log ratio), and on solar (+0.40) and mean temperature (+0.32; 0.83 below 8 MJ, 0.68 below 10 C): linear fit on ttf, canopy, T, VPD, solar, tmean explains 0.44 of the log-ratio variance, ttf alone 0.30. Above-ground only: law*fst (SI Eq. 8, fsti 0.45, fstf 0.95) over Cycles' above-ground gain 0.99 / 1.07 / 1.22 / 1.24 / 1.13 / 1.05 / 1.03; root: law*(1-fst) over Cycles' root gain 1.62 / 1.58 / 1.35 / 1.08 / 1.00 / 1.17 / 1.36. Cycles' realised shoot share of gain is 0.58 / 0.58 / 0.63 / 0.75 / 0.87 / 0.93 / 0.96 against the paper curve 0.45 / 0.48 / 0.59 / 0.77 / 0.89 / 0.93 / 0.94, i.e. shoot-heavier before 30 percent of thermal time. Reading: the partition early is not the SI curve with the file values (above-ground matches the law there, 0.99 to 1.07), but the largest remaining above-ground excess sits at 20 to 45 percent of thermal time (1.22 to 1.24) where the partition matches; no single simple rule explains both. So the stage term describes a departure of Cycles from its stated law that we have not explained; it is not established that a simpler mechanism exists. Open candidates to test from Cycles output: effective TUE and RUE against cold and dull days (solar below 12 MJ, tmean below 15 C), and the root accounting (turnover or rhizodeposit) in the first 20 percent of thermal time.

## 2026-10-18 effective TUE and RUE, and what Cycles does: law + SI.8 partition + 40 percent root loss (not adopted yet, clean forced-state match)
Effective TUE (Cycles gain / (TUE-law term)) on water-limited days and effective RUE on radiation-limited days: temperature and solar do not explain the stage pattern (log TUE ratio R2 0.18 with temperature, solar, VPD; 0.55 after adding thermal time; thermal time alone 0.43; effective TUE 6.7 / 6.8 / 7.2 / 7.8 / 8.1 / 8.4 at thermal-time fraction 0.05 / 0.15 / 0.25 / 0.38 / 0.53 / 0.7 against the file 8.7; effective RUE 1.89 to 2.11 against 2.2). Then the simple reading, checked on Cycles' own output: total gain = law * (shoot share + 0.6 * root share). With root retention 0.6 the law over Cycles' total gain is 0.98 / 1.02 / 1.07 / 1.09 / 1.06 / 1.03 / 1.02 by thermal-time fraction (1.25 to 1.28 early without it). Cycles' realised shoot share of net gain (above-ground gain over total gain) is fitted exactly by the SI Eq. 8 form fst = fsti + (fstf - fsti) / (1 + (TTf50 / TTf)^p) with the crop-file fsti 0.45 and fstf 0.95, p 4.3 (SI exponent 4), TTf50 0.420, root retention free 0.61: rms share error 0.0001 (0.0007 with retention fixed at 0.6) against 0.0585 for the engine's TTf50 0.32 (a 2026-09-23 refit). So the three sourced elements are: scalar 1.0, TTf50 0.42, root retention 0.6 (the 5/3 root input to soil). Bug found on the way: shoot_fraction bound ttf50=TTF50_SHOOT_PARTITION as a default argument at definition time, so overriding the module constant did nothing; it now reads the global at call time (no change to defaults). Test (corn, 24 sites, CWN = canopy, water and N factor forced, above-ground ratio engine/Cycles at N=0 / 150 / 400): current engine (TTf50 0.32, scalar 0.955) 0.980 / 1.043 / 1.038; TTf50 0.42, scalar 0.955 0.894 / 0.957 / 0.957; TTf50 0.42, scalar 1.0 0.936 / 1.002 / 1.002 (yield ratio 0.918 / 0.974 / 0.975). So the 'growth conversion 9 percent too high' and the 0.955 scalar were compensating the wrong TTf50 at N >= 150. Free run, TTf50 0.42 and scalar 1.0, paired against the current engine: MAE N=0 0.353 -> 0.457 (-0.104 [-0.182,-0.032], bias +0.054 -> +0.272), N=150 0.951 -> 0.948 (bias -0.076 -> +0.145), N=400 0.858 -> 0.880 (-0.022 [-0.061,+0.018], bias -0.140 -> -0.282), leaching rms log N=400 0.36 -> 0.31, N=0 0.44 -> 0.47, N=150 0.72 -> 0.80. The N=0 free over-yield (above-ground 1.137 free against 0.936 forced) is the separate N=0 error already found: the engine's mid-season N stress at N=0 is too weak (factor 0.493 against Cycles' 0.396 with equal plant N). Not adopted as default: TTF50 is a global constant shared by corn, soybean, sorghum and wheat and has not been checked per crop; ENGINE_OVR="TTF50_SHOOT_PARTITION=0.42" with NGF=1.0 reproduces the test.
Recheck (same day, after 'You sure?'): (1) the shoot-share fit is not in-sample: TTf50 0.422 and p 4.32 (rr 0.6; rr free 0.606, TTf50 0.420) are identical to three decimals on odd and even sites and at N=150 and N=400, because Cycles' partition is a deterministic function of thermal time; with p fixed at the SI value 4, TTf50 0.422. (2) The law table recomputed with TTf50 0.42 (scalar 1.0, retention 0.6, Cycles' own inputs): total gain over Cycles' 0.98 / 1.01 / 1.02 / 1.03 / 1.02 / 1.01 / 1.02 by thermal-time fraction at N=150 (all 1.020), N=400 all 1.030; above-ground 0.98 to 1.04 (all 1.019 / 1.030); with the old TTf50 0.32 above-ground was 1.22 to 1.24 at fractions 0.2 to 0.45. So the earlier 2 to 9 percent total figure was with the old partition; with 0.42 every stage is within 4 percent, leaving a flat +2 to 3 percent in the law itself (unexplained; candidates CO2 reference, RUE CO2 exponent). (3) Overstated earlier: the 'forced-state match' was above-ground only (1.002 at N >= 150); grain is still 2.5 percent low (yield ratio 0.974 / 0.975) and N=0 is 6 percent low even with canopy, water and N factor forced, so the harvest-index law (hi_slope 1.3077 etc., fitted under the old partition) and the N=0 state need refitting after the partition change. The cause given for the N=0 free-run over-yield (weak mid-season N stress) is a hypothesis from the earlier stress comparison, not tested with the new partition.

## 2026-10-18 adoption: TTf50 0.42 and growth scalar 1.0 for corn, silage corn, sorghum, soybean
Independent crop checks of TTf50 (SI form, exponent 4, crop-file fsti 0.45 / fstf 0.95, retention 0.6, Cycles output at N=150): corn 0.422, silage corn 0.422 (rms 0.0052), sorghum 0.419 (0.0041); retention free 0.63 and 0.62. TTf50 is not a crop-file field, so a single hard-coded value shared by crops makes sense; soybean and wheat Cycles output were not on disk to check. Forced-state (canopy, water, N factor forced, 24 sites) above-ground ratio engine/Cycles at N=0 / 150 / 400 with TTf50 0.42 and scalar 1.0: corn 0.936 / 1.002 / 1.002, silage 0.931 / 1.007 / 1.010, sorghum 0.945 / 1.002 / 0.999, soybean (N=0) 1.016; yield ratios corn 0.918 / 0.974 / 0.975, silage 0.953 / 0.977 / 0.977, sorghum 0.900 / 0.939 / 0.936, soybean 1.011. Harvest index: engine 0.976 to 0.981 of Cycles' at every N rate (corn site HI 0.445 / 0.474 / 0.488 against 0.454 / 0.487 / 0.500); the engine's HI law is the guide's with Hk = m(1-Hmin)/(Hmax-Hmin) = 1.3077 exactly; the crop-file slope 1.0 would give yield 0.857 of Cycles' and 1.479 gives 1.03, so the 2.5 percent is a residual in fG (above-ground growth after flowering over total), not a constant to refit. Code: shoot_fraction reads TTF50_SHOOT_PARTITION at call time and a per-crop ttf50 key (WHEAT carries 0.32); ngf_faithful 1.0 in run_validation.py (corn), run_validation_rotation2.py (soybean, silage corn) and run_validation_multisite.py (sorghum). 94 fresh sites, continuous chain, versus the same morning's configuration (TTf50 0.32, scalars 0.955 / 0.92): pooled correlation corn 0.951 / 0.924 / 0.962 -> 0.947 / 0.923 / 0.963, soybean 0.945 -> 0.943, silage 0.903 / 0.875 / 0.921 -> 0.900 / 0.885 / 0.927, sorghum 0.934 / 0.891 / 0.924 -> 0.930 / 0.888 / 0.923. Bias as percent of Cycles' mean: corn +18 / +4 / -4, silage +29 / +5 / 0, sorghum +11 / -1 / -9, soybean -8. Paired grain MAE gain (old minus new): corn -0.113 [-0.158,-0.072] / -0.020 [-0.067,+0.023] / -0.015 [-0.037,+0.005]; silage -0.212 [-0.289,-0.136] / +0.109 [+0.048,+0.174] / -0.000; sorghum -0.068 [-0.105,-0.031] / -0.022 / -0.035 [-0.052,-0.018]; soybean +0.008 [+0.003,+0.012]. Corn leaching rms log 0.61 / 0.72 / 0.37 -> 0.62 / 0.80 / 0.36. Pages re-synced, pattern checks 15/16 as before (the pages' own crop dictionaries carry no faithful keys, so they run the module defaults). Rollback: TTF50_SHOOT_PARTITION back to 0.32 and ngf_faithful back to 0.955 / 0.92.

## 2026-10-18 N=0 state comparison, free run, current engine (nstate_n0_2026_10_18.py, corn, 24 sites, 97,883 site-days, May to September means, Cycles / engine)
Nitrate pool kg/ha: 31.2 / 42.6 (May), 14.6 / 26.6 (June), 10.6 / 19.5 (July), 7.3 / 18.7 (Aug), 7.0 / 21.3 (Sept): the engine carries 1.4x Cycles' nitrate in May rising to 3x in September. N factor: 0.779 / 0.848, 0.458 / 0.597, 0.363 / 0.430, 0.431 / 0.427, 0.513 / 0.460: the engine is less stressed in May to July (largest in June, +0.14), equal in August. Canopy: 0.16 / 0.12, 0.28 / 0.26, 0.29 / 0.34, 0.25 / 0.38, 0.20 / 0.46: Cycles' canopy peaks in July and falls under N stress, the engine's keeps rising to 0.46 in September, which is where the over-yield forms. Plant N kg/ha: 8.7 / 7.3, 20.0 / 18.8, 30.4 / 31.5, 32.4 / 38.2, 46.3 / 58.4 (engine +26 percent by September); daily uptake 0.70 / 0.56, 0.45 / 0.52, 0.30 / 0.37, 0.26 / 0.33, 0.23 / 0.35; N concentration of biomass 2.60 / 2.83, 1.76 / 1.81, 1.35 / 1.28, 1.30 / 1.23, 1.18 / 1.10 percent. Reading: the N=0 over-yield is a late-season canopy and uptake excess that follows from a nitrate pool the engine keeps 1.4x (spring) to 3x (late summer) above Cycles'; the 3 percent per day uptake cap is not what limits the engine here (it takes up more than Cycles from June on). The pool excess is not explained by annual net mineralization (which matched in totals) or by leaching (the engine leaches more), so the open question is the monthly N input (net mineralization timing, spring carryover) versus Cycles'. Next measurement: dated monthly net mineralization, nitrification, leaching and denitrification, engine against Cycles' dailyN, at N=0.

## 2026-10-18 form of Cycles' composite decomposition factor (fe_form_2026_10_18.py, Cycles output only, corn N=150, 24 sites, 276k layer-1 days): hypothesis that the engine's fE is the N=0 problem, rejected
Cycles' composite_factor.1 is not separable (log-additive temperature x moisture) on saturation theta/theta_sat (R2 0.35), is on theta/fc (0.56), and is on relative wetness (theta - pwp)/(fc - pwp) (0.83; (theta-pwp)/(sat-pwp) 0.82), so the moisture axis is plant-available water, which is the axis the engine already uses. Moisture response h(relwet) on N-rich days below 24 C, normalised: 0.002 (-0.18), 0.08 (0.01), 0.15 (0.05), 0.37 (0.13), 0.51 (0.18, half response), 0.64 (0.22), 0.79 (0.32), 0.87 (0.45), 0.90 (0.53), 0.96 (0.87), 0.97 (1.0), 0.93 (1.2 to 1.4); disclosed DECOMP_HALF_RESP 0.22 and DECOMP_RESP_POWER 3.0 as x^3/(0.22^3 + x^3) misses it (rms 0.041; fitted half 0.17, power 1.8, rms 0.017), so the disclosed pair is not that function on this axis. Wet-soil temperature response of the factor: 0.025 (0 to 3 C), 0.13 (6 to 9), 0.31 (12 to 15), 0.54 (18 to 21), 0.77 (24 to 27), 0.86 (27 to 30). The engine's default per-layer path (`sixpool_fe_moisture_ml`, points refit 2026-10-02 on 104k Cycles layer-days: 0.04 at relwet 0, 0.13 at 0.05, 0.38 at 0.15, 0.79 at 0.30, 0.98 at 0.45, 0.97 at 1.0, 0.90 at 2.0) already follows this curve; the 0.157 floor I flagged belongs to the old non-per-layer function (`sixpool_fe_moisture`) which the default path does not use. So the environmental factor is not the cause of the nitrate excess at N=0; nothing changed in the engine. KD_NH4 5.6 (ammonium adsorption) is still not used by the engine and not yet tested.

## 2026-10-18 where the N=0 nitrate excess sits: year-round pool, layers and fallow fluxes (npool_year, nlayer, nflux scripts; corn N=0, 24 sites, free run, 1982 on)
Hooks added (default off): DIAG_NPOOL (end-of-day nitrate and ammonium, fallow-window days only; the in-season loop does not call the pool hook, so May to July have few rows), DIAG_NFLUX (fallow-window net mineralization, nitrification, volatilization, denitrification). Results. (1) The gap exists in January and all fallow months: nitrate Cycles / engine 20.2 / 29.7 (Jan), 21.5 / 31.7 (Apr), 11.2 / 20.0 (Aug), 15.1 / 23.5 (Oct), 19.1 / 28.1 (Dec); ammonium 3.5 / 2.5 (engine lower). (2) It is not a fading initial condition: engine minus Cycles nitrate (Oct to Apr) +14.4 (1982 to 85), +10.4, +8.5, +10.5, +9.7, +9.1, +7.8, +6.4, +6.1 (2014 to 17) while both pools decline, ratio 1.58 to 1.38 throughout; 88 percent of sites have the engine higher, per-site offset median +8.2, p10 -0.2, p90 +24.3. (3) It is in the topsoil: layer 1 10.0 / 15.3 kg/ha (1.53x), layer 2 4.1 / 7.8 (1.89x), deeper layers 5.7 / 6.0 (1.04x). (4) Fallow-season fluxes, Oct to Apr mean kg N/ha/day, Cycles / engine: net mineralization 0.071 / 0.086 (+21 percent), nitrification 0.079 / 0.077 (equal), volatilization 0.0030 / 0.0056 (1.9x), denitrification 0.0015 / 0.0052 (3.5x). In August Cycles' net mineralization is negative (-0.012, residue immobilization after early harvests) while the engine's is +0.155. Reading: nitrification and deep nitrate are not the problem; the engine already loses more N to denitrification and volatilization yet holds 1.5x to 1.9x more topsoil nitrate; with transport matched (nitrate leaves a layer in proportion to its saturated volume) the over-leaching of 1.5x equals the pool ratio, so the pool is the primary error and leaching follows it. A supply excess of about 21 percent net mineralization in the fallow season (about 3 kg N/ha over Oct to Apr) is the one measured surplus; it does not close the budget alone (leaching by day is not logged, and the in-season loop is not instrumented). Next measurement: gross mineralization and immobilization separately (Cycles dailyN has both; the engine's per-layer six-pool returns released and immobilized), to see whether the surplus is soil organic matter mineralization or too little residue immobilization.

## 2026-10-18 gross mineralization split and microbial C:N sensitivity (both uninformative or negative)
ngross_2026_10_18.py (fallow-window days, Oct to Apr, kg N/ha/day, corn N=0, 24 sites): Cycles gross mineralization 0.093, immobilization 0.022, net 0.071; engine release 0.182 (0.161 from soil organic matter and microbes, 0.020 from residue), immobilization 0.095, net 0.086. The engine's gross terms are 2x and 4x Cycles' with a similar net because the engine counts microbial turnover N in both release and immobilization, which Cycles' columns probably do not, so gross cannot be compared by these definitions; only net is comparable (+21 percent). August: Cycles gross 0.498 / immobilization 0.510 (net -0.012), engine 0.811 / 0.656 (net +0.155). Sensitivity of SI.14 (variable microbial C:N 8.5 to 11.25, engine constant 9.7): SIXPOOL_CN_CM 9.0 and 8.5 on 24 sites, grain MAE N=0 0.457 -> 0.472 (-0.015 [-0.023,-0.007]) and 0.485 (-0.028 [-0.042,-0.014]), N=150 and 400 unchanged, N=0 bias +0.272 -> +0.294 / +0.312, leaching unchanged: the wrong sign (the constant enters both release and immobilization, and a lower value raises release more) and small, so SI.14 as a constant change is not the fix. Budget inconsistency left open: fallow-season nitrification is equal (0.079 / 0.077), net mineralization is +21 percent, and the engine loses more to denitrification (3.5x) and volatilization (1.9x), and leaches more in total, yet holds 1.5x to 1.9x more topsoil nitrate; an earlier test that forced Cycles' net mineralization flux into the engine removed only 13 percent of the leaching error and 2 percent of the denitrification error. These do not add up, so either a flux or pool definition differs between the two models or a nitrate source in the engine is not logged. Next kind of step (two hypotheses rejected today: fE and microbial C:N): a mass-balance closure check at a few sites, pool change = inputs - outputs, in both models, day by day.

## 2026-10-18 (late): Cycles' supply-limited uptake law, N=0 corn, 24 sites (nuptake_law_2026_10_18.py)
61,338 supply-limited days (N stress > 30%). Daily uptake as a share of the previous day's profile mineral N rises with transpiration and saturates: median 0.014 at T<0.5 mm, 0.022 at 0.5-1, 0.029 at 1-2, 0.032 at 2-3, 0.033 at 3-4, 0.035 at 4-6 (p90 0.022 to 0.048). Uptake correlates best with T alone (0.64), then pool*sqrt(T) (0.57), pool*T/soil water (0.56), pool (0.48). So Cycles' ceiling is about 3 to 3.5% of the pool per day at full transpiration and lower on low-transpiration days; the engine's flat 3% cap ignores the T dependence. Caveats: low-T days are also early and late season (small crop), so the T effect is partly confounded with crop size; no fit adopted, nothing run in the engine yet.

## 2026-10-18 (late): in-season daily N flows, engine vs Cycles, N=0 corn, 24 sites (nseason_2026_10_18.py, hook DIAG_NSEASON)
99,668 in-season site-days, kg N/ha/day, Cycles | engine: net mineralization 0.219 | 0.206 (-6%), uptake 0.306 | 0.339 (+11%), leaching incl. bypass 0.0041 | 0.0069, denitrification 0.0018 | 0.0079, volatilization 0.020 | 0.014, mineral pool 18.2 | 26.7. In-season supply and total losses (0.026 | 0.029) are close, so no unlogged or double-counted in-season flow. The excess is the STARTING pool: May 27.5 | 36.6 (+33%), then the engine stays 8 to 9 kg/ha above Cycles all season (Jun 17.1 | 26.5, Aug 9.0 | 17.5) and takes up more in Jul to Sep (Sep 0.121 | 0.240 per day) because the pool is there. Late-season uptake after grain fill is a second, smaller question. Next: the Oct to Apr window (pool at Oct 1 versus May 1, fallow net mineralization was +21%, fallow losses), not the in-season flows. Denitrification is 4x high in season but is only 0.006 kg/ha/day against a pool gap of 8 to 9 kg/ha.

## 2026-10-18 (late): mineral N pool by month and fallow net mineralization (nfallow_2026_10_18.py, nflux.npy), N=0 corn, 24 sites
Engine pool / Cycles pool by month: Jan 1.36, Feb 1.35, Mar 1.34, Apr 1.37, Aug 1.61, Sep 1.49, Oct 1.41, Nov 1.37, Dec 1.36; absolute gap +8 to +9 kg/ha, set by August and flat all winter. The fallow does not accumulate it. The gap is a stationary level, so engine losses (annual 9.6 vs 5.9) and uptake (+11%) are consequences of the higher pool, not separate errors. Net mineralization, Cycles | engine (kg/ha/day): Jan 0.038 | 0.052, Feb 0.046 | 0.060, Mar 0.069 | 0.084, Apr 0.093 | 0.111, Oct 0.130 | 0.146, Nov 0.075 | 0.094, Dec 0.048 | 0.062: an additive offset of about +0.014/day (not a ratio), about +3 kg/ha over Oct to Apr. August is the large one: Cycles -0.012 (net immobilization) | engine +0.155, about +5 kg/ha over the month (fallow days after early harvests, n above 200 site-days; Sep 0.181 | 0.167 is fine). Candidates, in order: (1) post-harvest residue immobilization missing (residue C:N constant, audit hole 1); (2) additive cold-season mineralization offset. Not yet attributed; no engine change.

## 2026-10-18 (late): net mineralization by days since harvest, N=0 corn, 24 sites (npostharvest_2026_10_18.py)
Cycles | engine, kg/ha/day: day 1-7 -0.193 | +0.193, 8-14 +0.017 | +0.131, 15-30 0.116 | 0.097, 31-60 0.150 | 0.116, 61-100 0.097 | 0.105, 101-160 0.052 | 0.068, 161-240 0.072 | 0.093. Cumulative engine minus Cycles: +3.5 kg/ha by day 14 (the whole gap is a missing net-immobilization pulse in the first two weeks after harvest, Cycles goes strongly negative on days 1 to 7, the engine does not move off its in-season +0.19), -1.3 back by day 60 (engine slower in the following month), then +2.6 more over days 61 to 240 (the cold-season additive offset), +5.0 at day 240. Cycles' residue at harvest in these runs averages 1.67 Mg/ha, N 12.1 kg/ha, C:N 46.6 (harvest.csv matches dailyResidue); the engine's constant residue C:N of 86 would immobilize MORE, so the pulse is not a C:N problem, it is timing or what enters at harvest. To check next: what Cycles adds at harvest (dailyResidue root_residue and belowground residue jumps) versus the engine's daily root C input and `SIXPOOL_ROOT_HARVEST_FRAC`/`root_c_buffer`. No engine change yet.

## 2026-10-18 (late): post-harvest immobilization pulse found and adopted (CRA_MATURATION_TAU_DAYS 30 to 1)
Cycles' dailyResidue (Indiana-type site-year, harvest 1990-08-02): aboveground residue 1.49 Mg/ha with 9.5 kg N and root residue 0.63 Mg/ha with 2.7 kg N appear on harvest day, decay 4.7%/day immediately, immobilization jumps from -0.013 to -1.02 kg N/ha/day on day 0 while mineralization stays 0.29, and fades over about 10 days. The engine's fresh-residue lag (`cra_maturity_fraction`, tau 30 days, fitted 2026-10-02 on a binned ratio that MECHANISM_LOG 2026-10-07 already found disagreed with Cycles' own residue decay) removed that pulse. With tau 0.01, 24 sites N=0: net mineralization days 1-7 -0.150 (Cycles -0.193; was +0.193), cumulative engine minus Cycles +0.17 kg/ha at day 14 (was +3.5). Remaining: +4.6 at day 240, from the additive cold-season offset (days 61 to 240, about +0.02/day), not attributed yet.
94 fresh sites, corn, continuous chain, tau 1 versus tau 30 (same code otherwise): leaching rms log error 0.62 / 0.80 / 0.36 to 0.51 / 0.59 / 0.37 at N=0/150/400; means 7.6 / 21.0 / 97.8 to 6.7 / 18.3 / 94.0 kg N/ha (Cycles 4.6 / 13.3 / 84.8). Grain MAE 0.582 / 0.948 / 0.837 to 0.589 / 0.977 / 0.832, paired CIs all include zero (-0.007 [-0.026,+0.014], -0.029 [-0.063,+0.004], +0.006 [-0.020,+0.034]), bias +0.374 / +0.283 / -0.325 to +0.370 / +0.294 / -0.472. Adopted as a faithful mechanism fix (yield neutral, leaching clearly better, matches Cycles' own residue decay). Not re-run for soybean, silage corn, sorghum or wheat, which share the residue path; run them before the final report.
