## 2026-10-21 Silage residue return fixed (found while preparing cover crops)

The end-of-season residue credit was above-ground biomass minus GRAIN for every crop. Silage corn is clipped at 85 percent of maturity and Cycles removes 90 percent of the harvestable above-ground biomass (RESIDUE_REMOVED 90), so the engine returned about 50 percent of the above-ground biomass as residue where Cycles leaves about 5 to 10 percent. Fixed: residue = above-ground biomass minus what leaves the field, the forage for a crop harvested before maturity (harvest_ttf < 1), grain otherwise (grain crops unchanged). 94 sites, silage, held out, versus the previous engine: grain (forage) MAE 1.107 -> 0.978 [gain +0.128, +0.037 to +0.235] at N=0, 1.801 -> 1.605 [+0.196, +0.080 to +0.324] at N=150, 1.599 -> 1.610 [-0.010, -0.121 to +0.116] at N=400; pooled r 0.915 -> 0.932 / 0.898 -> 0.919 / 0.940 -> 0.954; leaching rms log 0.83 -> 0.58 / 1.19 -> 0.94 / 1.06 -> 0.47, level 1.71 -> 1.53 / 1.94 -> 1.82 / 1.42 -> 1.38; denitrification rms log 1.17 -> 0.70 / 1.33 -> 0.74 / 0.97 -> 0.60; N2O rms log 0.57 -> 0.33 / 0.79 -> 0.48 / 0.68 -> 0.69. Forage bias at N=400 is now -0.94 Mg/ha (was -0.29). Parity WORST 0.0.

## 2026-10-21 Cover crop feasibility (Cycles reference, 12 sites, 36 years)

`cover_ref_2026_10_21.py`: corn (continuous, N=0 and 150) plus WinterRye planted DOY 288 and killed by a Kill_Crop tillage at DOY 105, against the same site's corn alone. Rye above-ground biomass at kill 2.8 Mg/ha at N=0 and 3.0 at N=150 on average but strongly site dependent (0 to 0.3 at cold sites, 15.6 at the warmest, 2 to 5 at the middle sites; percentiles 5/50/95 = 0.0/1.2/15.2), shoot N 34 and 52 kg/ha. Effect on the following corn: yield -35 percent at N=0 (-0.62 Mg/ha), -3.7 percent at N=150 (-0.26), leaching -49 percent at both (-1.3 and -4.9 kg N/ha). Operations chain needed: fall planting, spring kill, shared soil state (the wheat chain in `run_validation_multisite_wheat.py` is the template: a day-indexed weather list, fallow windows, `simulate_season` over a row slice).

## 2026-10-21 Cover crop in the season chain (first implementation)

Engine: `_season_chain(..., cover=dict(crop=, plant_doy=, kill_doy=))` and `simulate_continuous(..., cover=)`. The cover crop is its own `simulate_season` over the rows from its planting day (after the main crop's last day) to the kill day of the next year, on the shared layers, mineral N and six-pool state; its season losses are added to the year it ends in (calendar-year attribution is approximate), `result["cover"]` carries biomass, total N uptake and leaching. A crop flagged `residue_n_from_plant` returns its above-ground plant N with its residue (surface residue C:N becomes a blended state `cra_cn`; crops without the flag keep the constant 86, bit-identical regression, parity WORST 0.0). Rye = the winter-wheat machinery (`r2.WHEAT`, vernalization) with the two WinterRye crop-file values that differ (flowering at Pmax 290, transpiration threshold 8 C), harvest index 0, no harvest. Harness `cover_chain_2026_10_21.py` against the Cycles reference of `cover_ref_2026_10_21.py` (12 sites, 36 years, rye DOY 288, kill DOY 105).

Results (engine | Cycles): without the plant-N residue link, rye shoots at kill N=0 2.84 | 2.76 Mg/ha (year corr 0.98, per-site pattern right from 15 at the warmest site to 0 at cold ones), N=150 3.96 | 3.03; corn yield change -1.36 | -0.62 (N=0), -1.65 | -0.26 (N=150); leaching change -2.3 | -1.3 and -8.5 | -4.9 kg N/ha. With the link (rye residue C:N from plant N instead of 86): rye 3.28 | 2.76 and 4.49 | 3.03, corn yield change -0.85 | -0.62 and -0.77 | -0.26, leaching change -1.9 | -1.3 and -7.4 | -4.9. The link is the physically required one (the 86 over-immobilized rye residue) and brings the yield penalty close at N=0; rye then grows more in the engine because returned N feeds later years.
Open: at N=150 Cycles' rye is SMALLER than at N=0 at several sites (f021 4.5 against 6.1 Mg/ha; its daily output reports higher nitrogen stress and lower radiation interception at N=150: immobilization by the larger corn residue after a fertilized crop), whereas the engine's rye grows more at N=150; same direction as the engine's mineral pool being 1.3x Cycles' after corn. Not yet addressed.

## 2026-10-21 Cover crop on 24 sites; simulator option

24 sites, 36 years (1983 on), corn with cereal rye (plant DOY 288, kill DOY 105), engine | Cycles. N=0: rye shoots at kill 2.48 | 2.19 Mg/ha (year corr 0.98), corn yield change -0.70 | -0.55 Mg/ha, leaching change -1.4 | -0.9 kg N/ha; N=150: rye 3.77 | 2.29 (year corr 0.87), yield change -0.84 | -0.25, leaching change -4.7 | -2.9; the engine planted no rye in 2 site-years at each N (corn still growing at DOY 288, where Cycles plants into the living corn). Cycles itself: corn yield -37 percent at N=0 and -3.5 percent at N=150, leaching -41 and -47 percent. Reading: the pattern and the N=0 numbers are right; at N=150 the engine's rye grows about 65 percent too much and costs corn too much yield, the same state problem as the engine's post-harvest mineral N pool (Cycles' rye is held back by immobilization and low nitrate after the fertilized corn).
Interface: `engine_api.py` gains a Cover crop group (none or cereal rye, planting day, kill day; corn, silage corn, sorghum only; kill must precede the earliest planting day) and two annual columns (cover crop biomass at kill, cover crop N uptake) when a cover crop is on. `check_parity_2026_10_19.py` now includes corn plus rye at N=0 and 150: WORST 0.0 (API against the chain called directly). Not yet tested in a browser.

## 2026-10-21 (late): management responses against Cycles (`mgmt_response_2026_10_21.py`)
24 sites, continuous corn, change from baseline (engine | Cycles), per site-year, about 800 site-years each. Sources checked: Cycles `input.md` (operations, AUTO_IRRIGATION, tillage), `tillage_tools.txt`, `fertilizers.txt`, engine tillage and manure code.
- Late planting (window +25 d, N=150): grain -0.35 | -0.20 (year corr of change 0.41), leaching -0.35 | -0.12, volatilization +1.5 | +1.2. Acceptable.
- Auto irrigation (50% depletion): grain +2.77 | +2.15 (corr 0.86), volatilization -6.5 | -6.1 (0.89); WRONG SIGN on denitrification +4.6 | -2.7, N2O +0.4 | -0.8, leaching +0.56 | -0.27. Engine adds a fixed 25 mm per event, Cycles refills to field capacity; the sign errors may be the extra yield not drawing nitrate down (3% uptake cap) or the amount semantics. Untested which.
- Manure (N=0 plus Dairy_Manure, 150 kg total N, 3947 kg/ha): grain +1.41 | +4.85, leaching +0.44 | +3.58, denitrification +2.0 | +11.3, N2O +0.31 | +1.50, volatilization +2.2 | +10.9. Cause: the engine credits only the NH4 fraction (18% of the N) and drops the organic N (82%) and the 43% carbon; Cycles decomposes them. Largest hole found.
- Tillage (planter vs chisel plus planter; the engine baseline previously had no planter pass, now added in the test only): grain +0.10 | -0.39 (corr 0.04), denitrification +1.2 | +5.3, volatilization +0.4 | -1.0. Engine mixes moisture and solutes and applies the decomposition acceleration, but does not mix residue and carbon pools between layers (Cycles MIXING_EFFICIENCY, `input.md` line 677). Not yet implemented.
- Soil carbon response not yet measured. Order of fixes: manure organic N and C into the six-pool state, then tillage residue and pool mixing, then irrigation refill-to-field-capacity and its nitrate sign.

## 2026-10-21 (late): manure organic N and carbon
Change: with `manure_source`, the organic fraction (norg_frac of the N, carbon from the catalog's C_Organic over N_Organic, `c_per_norg`) enters the surface residue pool with a blended C:N; the NH4 fraction was already credited. 24 sites, N=0 plus Dairy_Manure 150 kg N, change from N=0 (engine | Cycles): grain +5.15 | +4.85 (was +1.41), volatilization +10.1 | +10.9 (was +2.2), leaching +7.0 | +3.6 (was +0.4), denitrification +22 | +11 (was +2.0), N2O +3.1 | +1.5; year corr of change 0.86 / 0.80 / 0.72 / 0.64 / 0.92. Yield and volatilization now match; leaching, denitrification and N2O responses overshoot about 2x, the same ratio as the engine's known N=0 denitrification bias (baseline 1.69 vs 0.59), so not attributed to manure. Non-manure runs untouched (guarded). The saved snapshot `snap_res.json` was already stale at HEAD before this change (38 cases DIFFERENT with the change stashed); new snapshot `/tmp/w/snap_man.json`. Parity WORST 0.0.

## 2026-10-21 (late): tillage response
Result: NOT closed. Two input bugs fixed, one mechanism left open.
- Fixed: the tillage decomposition speed-up (`tillage_ftx`) used the default topsoil clay 0.21 in every run, so sandy sites (clay 1.5%, ftx 4.7 instead of 2.3) got half the speed-up; `engine_api` now passes the site's topsoil clay. The API also never passed a manure species, so the 2026-10-21 organic-manure path was unreachable from the simulator; a `manure_source` select (default dairy) was added. Parity WORST 0.0.
- Tried and reverted: moving MIXING_EFFICIENCY of the surface residue carbon into the tilled layers' residue pool. No improvement (denitrification response +0.16 vs Cycles +5.3).
- What Cycles does (f009_335_945, daily output, chisel+planter vs planter only): the response is concentrated in a few sites (denitrification +32, +26, +19, +14, +14 kg N/ha at 5 of 24 sites, grain -2.3 to -0.2, the engine sees +0.7 to +1.6 and +0.0 to +0.5). Net mineralization at an ordinary site rises only 3 to 9% (engine, with the clay fix, a similar size), so the big effect is elsewhere. At f009 Cycles' surface residue is 4.8 Mg/ha on 1 October with chisel and 2.8 with planter only, and 3.0 against 0.2 on 20 April (winter loss 36% against 92%), with belowground residue 0.9 against 0.02 right after the chisel pass, residue evaporation 2x, and layer-1 water higher. So tillage history changes the surface residue decay over the following winter, a mechanism not in the paper text read so far and not identified. Next step if pursued: read the SI residue decomposition section for a tillage or contact term, and check Cycles' residue pools on a site pair before touching code.

## 2026-10-21 (late): tillage mixes the soil, not only the moisture
Sources checked: Kemanian et al. 2024 Sec. 2.6 (tillage "mixes all soil layer attributes based on a soil mixing efficiency coefficient", "homogenize soil texture in the tilled layers"), SI Fig. SI.2 (tillage first in the daily order, "changes many soil state variables"), Cycles `input.md`, and Cycles daily output on matched chisel-plus-planter vs planter-only runs at f009, f051 (`dailySoilLayersCN`, `dailyResidue`, `dailyWater`, `dailyN`, `annualSOM`).
What Cycles does (measured): layer carbon, bulk density and thickness mix across the tilled depth (f051 layer-1 stabilized C 62.7 to 28.2 and layer 2 10.6 to 45.1 over 15 years; layer mass thickness x BD conserved); the effect accumulates over years; surface residue is incorporated (belowground residue 0.93 vs 0.02 Mg/ha right after the pass at f009), which raises immobilization (f051 -30 to -146 kg N/yr) and cuts net mineralization 37 to 41%. At sites whose layers differ in texture (f009: sandy loam over clay) texture mixing changes layer-1 water retention, which is what slowed surface residue decay and raised denitrification there (the 2026-10-21 puzzle; it was soil mixing, not a residue-decay term).
Engine change (all three only run when a tillage implement is set; `tillage_mix_texture=True` default): `mix_tilled_layers` also blends sand and clay (kept on the layer dict so passes accumulate; fc, pwp, sat, ksat, psi_e, B recomputed; each layer keeps its own SOC for the pedotransfer, because Cycles' water content did not follow the mixed organic matter at f051); the per-layer carbon pools cs, cm, crtz, crm, csx mix by the same coefficient over the same partial-layer overlap; MIXING_EFFICIENCY of the surface residue carbon moves into the tilled layers' residue pool. Whole-layer mixing was tried and rejected: partial overlap reproduces Cycles' carbon profile (f051 layer 1: engine 40.9 / 34.6 / 32.1 vs Cycles 41.8 / 32.4 / 28.2 at years 5 / 10 / 15; f009 16.5 / 15.8 / 16.1 vs 17.2 / 15.9 / 16.0). `engine_api` passes the site's topsoil clay (the default 0.21 halved the speed-up on sandy soils).
Result, 24 sites, chisel plus planter vs planter (engine | Cycles): year correlation of the change rose from about 0 to grain 0.34 (was 0.01), leaching 0.51 (0.39), denitrification 0.33 (-0.08), N2O 0.48 (-0.01), volatilization 0.81 (0.22); volatilization change -0.74 | -1.02. Net mineralization change at f051 matches (engine -34 / -49 / -35%, Cycles -37 / -41 / -38%).
Still wrong: the mean denitrification change (engine -0.5, Cycles +5.3, driven by f009 +32, f030 +26, f039 +19, f015 +14, f018 +14) and the mean grain change (-0.07 | -0.39). The engine's denitrification law (layer respired carbon x nitrate x saturation) responds to layer-1 carbon that mixing removes, and at carbon-stratified uniform-texture sites (f048, f051) it falls 45 to 50% while Cycles falls 10 to 30%. The carbon term of that law is a proxy, not Cycles' rule; the next step on this thread is the denitrification law itself, not tillage.
Regression: non-tillage runs unchanged (snapshot IDENTICAL except the tillage case and the API manure case, both intended), parity WORST 0.0, new snapshot `/tmp/w/snap_till.json`.

## 2026-10-21 (late): irrigation
Source: Cycles `input.md` AUTO_IRRIGATION (trigger when the fraction of plant available water depleted over the layers down to LAST_SOIL_LAYER exceeds WATER_DEPLETION; the event adds the water that brings those layers to field capacity; START and STOP day).
Engine change: `irrigation_refill_layers` and `irrigation_window` implement that rule (the old fixed 25 mm event stays when they are not set). `engine_api` now passes the trigger as 1 - depletion (the label said depletion, the engine read it as availability, so any value but 0.5 did the opposite), refill depth, and a start and stop day.
Result, 24 sites, 50% depletion, refill to about 1 m, 150 to 260 (engine | Cycles; before the change in brackets): grain +2.67 | +2.15, year corr 0.89 (0.86); volatilization -4.4 | -6.1 corr 0.87; leaching +0.83 | -0.27 corr 0.53 (0.38); denitrification +2.9 | -2.7 corr 0.31 (0.19), N2O +0.14 | -0.80 corr 0.37 (0.23). Irrigation volume 249 vs 170 mm/yr on 12 sites (1.46x; the engine's known high evaporation and over-stressed water balance, not irrigation's own rule). Crop N: Cycles N in harvest 97 to 112, engine uptake 172 to 197.
Open: the sign of the denitrification response (Cycles falls 23% with irrigation, engine rises 18%). Same suspect as the tillage and manure results: the denitrification law (nitrate x layer respired carbon x layer-1 saturation) is a fitted proxy, and every management test that changes layer saturation or carbon moves it differently from Cycles. Next step: refit the law against Cycles' daily output across these treatments, not only the baseline.

## 2026-10-21 (late): denitrification law under management, negative result and redirect
Question: is the wrong-sign denitrification response (irrigation, tillage, manure) caused by the fitted law? Method: daily Cycles states under base and irrigated runs at 24 sites (`denit_mgmt_data_2026_10_21.py`: layer 1 and 2 nitrate, moisture, saturation, respiration, denitrification), law evaluated on Cycles' own states (`denit_mgmt_eval_2026_10_21.py`), refit with odd/even site hold-out (`denit_refit_2026_10_21.py`). Sources: Cycles `output.md` (layer NO3 columns are mg/kg, not kg/ha; an earlier version of this diagnostic read them as mass and gave a false 5x nitrate gap, caught against the profile total at f009: 35 mg/kg x 2.25 + 8 x 8.3 + 2 x 6.4 = 158 vs reported 159).
Findings: (1) layer-1 law on Cycles' states reproduces the baseline level (15.1 vs 15.4 kg N/ha/yr) but gives -3% under irrigation where Cycles gives -18% (15.4 to 12.7); (2) a layer-1 form refit on base plus irrigated data cannot produce the fall either (held-out change -0.4 vs -1.7); (3) a two-layer form (layer 2 respiration share fitted, 0.19) does: fitted on the baseline alone the held-out change is -0.9, fitted on base plus irrigated -1.0 (Cycles -1.7), held-out R2 0.88 to 0.90 against 0.69 to 0.85 for the layer-1 forms; level 17.9 vs 16.5; (4) the engine's drivers under irrigation track Cycles': layer-1 moisture +0.040 vs +0.041, respiration +26% vs +21%, layer-1 nitrate -52% vs -60%, layer-2 nitrate -66% vs -73%; (5) but the engine's in-season layer nitrate is 1.5x (layer 1, 116 vs 77 kg/ha) and 2.1x (layer 2, 82 vs 39) Cycles', which puts the nitrate term c/(c+kh) at a flatter part of its curve (engine base c about 52 mg/kg, Cycles about 34, kh 31.5), so the nitrate fall cannot offset the +20 to +26% respiration and wetter soil.
Conclusion: the law structure is adequate; the wrong sign comes from the engine's excess in-season nitrate, the same open item as the post-harvest and July-August nitrate floor (plant N access capped at 3% of the pool per day, ENGINE_REBUILD_PLAN 2026-10-18). Law refit NOT adopted (held-out gain small and its form needs a profile-total respiration the engine does not have). Next: the in-season nitrate budget by layer (nitrification placement, uptake access) before any further law work.
`record_history` now also returns `no3_layers` (diagnostic, no behaviour change; parity WORST 0.0).

## 2026-10-21 (late): in-season nitrate budget, Stage 1 (`nbudget_2026_10_21.py`, `nbudget_engine_2026_10_21.py`)
Data: Cycles daily N pools, fluxes and plant N (`dailyN`, `dailyCornRM.90`) at 24 sites, N=0 and 150, 1983 to 2015, against the engine's season history (24 sites, same years). Sources: Cycles `output.md` (plant N columns, `-999` when no crop), the 2026-10-18 notes on the 3% uptake cap.
- Cycles' calendar-year budget closes: N=0 residual -2.6 kg N/ha/yr (sd 3.1) against flows of about 40; N=150 residual -15 (about 10% of the fertilizer rate, unexplained: not leaching, denitrification, volatilization or bypass, all counted).
- Planting dates agree (day 116 in both).
- The engine takes up MORE N than Cycles, not less: final above-ground N 168 vs 141 kg/ha at N=150 (corr 0.85), 39 vs 33 at N=0 (corr 0.97); during the season 4 to 12% more at N=150 (+19% by harvest). So the 3% cap is not starving the crop relative to Cycles; the earlier reading that uptake access is the missing equation is not supported.
- Yet the engine carries 1.3 to 1.5x the nitrate on crop-alive days (N=150, days 150 to 249: NO3 307 / 266 / 236 / 228 / 194 vs 231 / 188 / 159 / 160 / 149; NH4 is lower, 5 to 8 vs 12 to 26, because nitrification is faster).
- The surplus is inherited. Mineral pool at planting: N=0 engine 36 vs Cycles 28 (+32%); N=150 engine 369 on the first season day vs Cycles 312 on planting day (Cycles pool the day before planting 162, +150 fertilizer; engine pre-plant about 219, +35%). N=0 split (744 site-years): end of the engine season (day 233) 17.7 vs 12.3 (+5.4), off-season change +18.1 vs +15.5 (+2.6, Cycles net mineralization +18.5 and losses 2.6).
Reading: the in-season uptake rule is not the lever. Two candidates: (1) the engine's plant N is 18 to 19% above Cycles' at similar biomass (N concentration too high, which also feeds residue N; AUDIT item 0b(1): the plant-to-residue N link is open); (2) the pool carried from season end through winter (+5 at end of season, +3 over winter, plus 35% at N=150). Stage 2 as scoped (fit Cycles' uptake rule) is dropped; revised next step is to compare above-ground N concentration by day against Cycles' `aboveground_nitrogen_concentration` and the Cycles end-of-season to planting pool chain at N=150.

## 2026-10-21 (late): lost-root N sink adopted; Stage 1 corrections
Corrections to the Stage 1 entry above: (a) the engine's history `n_plant` is whole-plant N (above-ground is 0.88 of it), Cycles' `agN` is above-ground only, so "the engine takes up 19% more N" was a definition error; like for like plant N is +4% at N=150 (168 vs 161) and +7% at N=0; (b) `n_uptake_rate = 0.03` is set in the corn, silage and sorghum crop dicts, so the 3% per day cap IS active in the validated runs (the module default None is overridden), while uptake still matches Cycles within 4 to 7%; (c) Cycles' crop N concentration on unstressed days is a constant 0.69 to 0.77 of the crop-file curve a B^-0.4 at every biomass class (`ncurve_2026_10_21.py`), so `N_DEMAND_SCALE` 0.70 is a measured property of Cycles' demand curve, not a fudge.
Found: in Cycles' calendar-year budget the mineral pool loses 0.082 (N=0) and 0.093 (N=150) x the plant N gain beyond what the plant keeps (residual vs plant N, corr -0.97 / -0.91, 24 sites, 33 years). Cause: the share of root growth that leaves the plant (1 - root_retained_frac = 0.4) carries N taken from the soil, returned to it as organic N; at the retained roots' concentration the sink is (1 - rr)/rr x (1 - N_AG_FRACTION) x uptake = 8.0% of uptake (the new-growth concentration would give 14 to 15%, too high). The engine never debited it, while its residue bookkeeping releases the N later: a source of mineral N from nothing, so the pool inflated until losses balanced.
Change: `RHIZO_N_UPTAKE` (default on, env `RHIZO_N=0` switches off): that N is removed from the mineral pools on the day of uptake, not added to plant N; no new constant. Applies only where `root_retained_frac` < 1 (corn, silage corn, sorghum).
Result, 94 fresh sites, continuous chain, same code off vs on (paired, grain MAE gain [CI], pooled r, leaching level engine/Cycles):
- corn N=0 +0.078 [+0.051,+0.109], bias +0.26 to +0.08, r 0.954 to 0.959, leaching 1.35 to 1.24; N=150 +0.018 [-0.016,+0.053], bias +0.17 to -0.06, r 0.923 to 0.925, leaching 1.34 to 1.21; N=400 neutral, leaching 1.22 to 1.10.
- silage N=0 +0.115 [+0.069,+0.166], r 0.932 to 0.938, leaching 1.53 to 1.42; N=150 -0.033 [-0.067,+0.001] (not significant, bias -0.22 to -0.43), r 0.919 to 0.921, leaching 1.82 to 1.64; N=400 neutral.
- sorghum N=0 +0.051 [+0.024,+0.079], r 0.934 to 0.939; N=150 +0.030 [-0.003,+0.065] (bias -0.15 to -0.36); N=400 neutral.
Denitrification and N2O also move toward Cycles at every N rate for the three crops (corn N=0 denitrification 2.71x to 2.47x, rms log 0.76 to 0.69). Nothing significantly worse; the more negative N=150 bias for silage and sorghum is the N stress calibration, tuned while the sink was missing, now showing.
Pools (24 sites, season window): end-of-season mineral pool at N=150 170 vs Cycles 152 (was 211); N=0 15.2 vs 12.5 (was 17.9). Management responses (24 sites): irrigation denitrification response +2.5 vs Cycles -2.7 (was +2.9): the sign is still wrong; manure leaching response +6.2 vs +3.6 (was +7.0). Remaining nitrate surplus is about +12% (N=150) and +20% (N=0) at season end; next: the winter and start-of-season pool chain, and the N=150 N stress calibration for silage and sorghum.

## 2026-10-21 (late): surplus nitrate, off-season chain, retained roots at harvest (flag `ROOT_AT_HARVEST`, default off pending the 94-site pairs)
Question: where does the remaining nitrate surplus (N=0 start of season +5.7 kg N/ha, N=150 end of season +18) come from. Sources checked: Cycles daily output (`dailyN`, `dailySoilC`, `dailyResidue`, `dailySoilLayersCN`, `dailyEnviron`), plan 2026-10-18 and Stage 1 entries.
Findings (24 sites, N=0, `offseason_*_2026_10_21.py`): annual net mineralization matches (45.1 vs 44.3 kg N/ha) and so do soil temperature (within 0.4 C every month) and surface residue carbon (within 1 to 3%), but the timing is off: engine respiration +38 to 60% in June and July, net mineralization -10 to -28% in May and June and +25% from October to March. Cause: the engine fed the retained roots' carbon (the 5/3 root input includes the retained 0.6) into the decaying residue pool every day while the roots were alive; Cycles keeps them in the plant and moves them to root residue at harvest (`dailyResidue`: root residue 0.4 to 0.6 Mg/ha through the season, 2.97 after harvest at f009). In-soil residue carbon engine vs Cycles: June 0.242 vs 0.144, January 0.088 vs 0.152 before the change.
Change: with `root_retained_frac` < 1 only the lost share (1 - rr) of the root input enters the pool daily; the retained share accumulates and is credited to the layers' root residue pool at the end of the crop, with the same depth weights. This also removes the early release of root N that the plant still held (the retained roots' N was debited as plant uptake). Env `ROOT_AT_HARVEST=1` switches on.
Result so far (24 sites, N=0): in-soil residue matches Cycles in every month (January 0.159 vs 0.152, June 0.146 vs 0.144), respiration June 0.191 vs 0.187 (was 0.258), June net N 7.01 vs 6.99 (was 5.02), annual respiration 1.46 vs 1.40 (was 1.52), annual net N 44.4 vs 44.3; start-of-season pool 30.6 vs Cycles 28.3 (was 34.0); N=150 end-of-season pool unchanged (170 vs 152). Open: the harvest-month dip (August net N 1.2 vs 3.5, September 4.3 vs 5.6, then October to March +2.9 above Cycles: the pulse at harvest immobilizes more and releases later than Cycles), and winter respiration still +36%.
Held-out result for `ROOT_AT_HARVEST` (94 sites, same code off vs on, RHIZO on in both, paired grain MAE gain [CI]): corn N=0 -0.029 [-0.048,-0.011], N=150 -0.089 [-0.143,-0.039] (bias -0.06 to +0.20), N=400 neutral; sorghum N=0 -0.044 [-0.066,-0.025], N=150 -0.155 [-0.233,-0.086] (bias -0.36 to -0.03, pooled r 0.889 to 0.869), N=400 neutral; silage N=0 +0.025 [+0.005,+0.046], N=150 +0.049 [+0.025,+0.077], N=400 neutral. Leaching and denitrification improve for all three (corn leaching 1.24/1.21/1.10 to 1.09/1.09/1.06 of Cycles, rms log 0.46/0.50/0.25 to 0.38/0.42/0.24; sorghum 1.27/1.18/1.08 to 1.09/1.08/1.05). NOT ADOPTED (default stays off): corn and sorghum grain are significantly worse, the corn and sorghum yield now over-shoots at N=150. Reading: the states are now right (residue pools, respiration, net N by month, leaching) and supply to the crop in the season matches Cycles', so the over-yield is the N stress and uptake side, which was calibrated while supply was too low (the known mid-season N stress 0.493 vs 0.396 at N=0 with equal plant N). Next step is that calibration, measured against Cycles' daily N stress with the corrected pools, then turn this on.

## 2026-10-22: N stress law refit and retained roots at harvest, held-out result (94 sites, paired grain MAE gain vs the adopted state RHIZO on, roots daily)
Law check first (no refit before this): the engine's N stress law applied to Cycles' own daily plant states reproduces Cycles' N factor with a small bias (mean 0.507 vs 0.483 at N=0, 0.857 vs 0.840 at N=150, 0.983 vs 0.987 at N=400; corr 0.87 / 0.95 / 0.83), equal to the engine's excess N factor at N=150 (+0.017 to +0.026 at equal plant N 148 vs 147). Sources checked: Kemanian 2024 Sec 2.5 (no stress equation), SI (none), CropSyst 1994 Eq 29 and 30 (linear N-limited growth with deficiency demand; Cycles states no compensatory uptake), Lemaire 2008 NNI (index, no response curve). No source gives Cycles' stress or root N access equations, so the law stays a measured one. Refit (same form) on Cycles' states, odd/even site hold-out: (SMAX, R0, R1, Q) 0.764, 0.641, 0.281, 0.90 to 0.794, 0.621, 0.247, 0.668; held-out bias at N=150 +0.015 to 0.000, mae 0.040 to 0.033 (flag `NSTRESS_REFIT`).
Refit alone: corn N=0 +0.015 [+0.004,+0.026], N=150 -0.012 [-0.027,+0.003]; silage N=0 +0.042 [+0.020,+0.067], N=150 -0.026 [-0.041,-0.013] (bias -0.43 to -0.50); sorghum neutral. Refit plus `ROOT_AT_HARVEST`: corn N=0 -0.009 (n.s.), N=150 -0.068 [-0.113,-0.025] (bias +0.12, r 0.925 to 0.917); silage +0.061 [+0.025,+0.101] / +0.032 [+0.009,+0.059]; sorghum -0.030 [-0.048,-0.013] / -0.128 [-0.195,-0.070] (r 0.889 to 0.873). Leaching and denitrification improve with roots at harvest in every crop. NOT ADOPTED (both flags stay off): silage N=150 is significantly worse with the refit alone, corn and sorghum N=150 significantly worse with roots at harvest. Per rule 6 the next step changes kind: not another refit; the N=150 grain response to supply (corn bias moves -0.16 to +0.12 across these configurations while pooled correlation falls with roots at harvest) points at the supply side (uptake access, harvest-month immobilization pulse), where the engine's cap is the unmeasured piece.

## 2026-10-22: CropSyst Eq. 26 uptake form against Cycles' daily uptake (`uptake_data_2026_10_22.py`, `uptake_forms_2026_10_22.py`)
Sources: CropSyst 1994 (Stockle, Martin, Campbell, `cropsyst.pdf`, Eqs. 25, 26, 29, 30; the availability functions are "given by Godwin and Jones 1991", not in the repo), Kemanian 2024 Sec 2.5 and 2.7, Cycles daily output at 24 sites (N=0 and 150, 223,896 site-days: plant N gain, layer nitrate, ammonium, moisture, transpiration, biomass, N stress).
Test: on days when Cycles' crop is short of N (N factor < 0.6, 61,141 days), explain daily uptake with candidate supply forms, fit on odd sites and test on even sites.
- Typical size: uptake is 2.4% of the mineral pool per day (median), but p10 0.1% and p90 4.1%.
- Daily test R2: cap k x pool 0.04; k x pool x root/(root + h) 0.20; mass flow (transpiration x solution concentration) -0.51; Eq. 26 shape (root length x (1 - exp(-a (c - xmin))) x SWF^2, depth-weighted) -1.83.
- Weekly sums (10,937 stretches of 7 stressed days): cap -0.48; pool x root saturation 0.35; pool x moisture -0.60; transpiration x pool 0.29; demand of Cycles' actual growth (retained biomass gain x marginal concentration) 0.76; sqrt(pool x demand) 0.81.
- Relation to growth: on stressed days uptake is about 26% of the demand of the growth Cycles actually makes (62% on unstressed days, which is the 0.70 curve times the retained share); the new-growth concentration relative to 0.70 x the marginal curve is 0.3 to 0.45 where the plant N status r (concentration over critical) is below about 0.6, and 0.9 to 1.07 above 0.6, the same threshold as the stress law's R0 (0.62 to 0.64).
Conclusion: none of the documented or standard soil-supply forms (CropSyst Eq. 26 shape, mass flow, the engine's pool fraction) explains Cycles' daily uptake; the soil pool explains little of it in the stressed regime, uptake tracks the growth made at a reduced new-growth concentration. This is again a measured relation, not a documented one. NEGATIVE for the CropSyst route. No engine change.

## 2026-10-22: the preserved Cycles-L source (Zenodo 8087162 and 7942338) does not contain the crop and N code
Matt authorised reading it. Contents checked: 8087162 is MM-PIHM v1.0.0 (MIT licence): the PIHM hydrology plus a 879-line `src/cycles` coupling (daily call into the Cycles library, per-layer sink and source bookkeeping, lateral solute transport); its Makefile compiles against an external `Cycles_dev/src` (version 0.13.0-alpha.post) that is not in the archive and is private (GitHub returns 403). 7942338 is the Cycles-L v1.0.0 executable only. The public PSUmodeling/Cycles repository holds docs and release binaries, no source. Nothing in them gives the crop, uptake, N stress or decomposition code. Only fact gained: uptake is carried per layer and separately for nitrate (`no3_uptake`) and ammonium (`nh4_uptake`), alongside per-layer `nitrif`, `denitrif`, `volatil`, `n2o_from_nitrif`, `no3_immobil`, `nh4_immobil`, `mineral`. No Cycles crop code was read; the downloaded archives were deleted. The independent-build position is unchanged.

## 2026-10-22: wheat gets the lost-root N sink (adopted)
Found: Cycles' wheat calendar-window N budget (8 sites, N=0, 208 site-years, Oct 20 to Jul 31) loses 0.057 x plant N beyond the plant's own (corr -0.98). Structural prediction with no fitted number: (1 - rr)/rr x root N share = 0.4/0.6 x 0.082 = 0.055 (wheat root N share read from `dailyWinterWheat`: 0.082, sd 0.008; root biomass share 0.19). The 40% root loss is a rule for every crop. Wheat keeps its tuned growth path (`root_retained_frac` unset), so the sink uses its own keys `rhizo_rr` 0.6 and `rhizo_root_n_share` 0.082 (harness `WHEAT_RHIZO`, default on; `engine_api._WHEAT` carries the same keys, rye pops them and is unchanged).
Wheat before: at N=0 the engine's plant N was 94 vs Cycles 72 kg/ha (+31%) with equal net mineralization (49 vs 48), N=150 220 vs 195 (+13%); engine nitrification 22 vs 49 (N=0) and 127 vs 153 (N=150).
Result, 58 sites, paired grain MAE gain vs the pre-change wheat run [CI], pooled r: N=0 +0.028 [+0.004,+0.052], bias +0.149 to +0.011, r 0.927 to 0.929; N=90 +0.088 [+0.054,+0.124], bias +0.434 to +0.250, r 0.841 to 0.851; N=150 -0.005 [-0.036,+0.027], bias +0.056 to -0.099, r 0.861 to 0.863. ADOPTED. Wheat N losses are still not recorded by the wheat harness. Open for wheat: the cold-season nitrification law (engine 45% of Cycles' at N=0), the southern sites (f070, f033: Cycles 2 to 4 Mg/ha at N=0 with large year-to-year swings, engine 6 to 7).

## 2026-10-22: cold-season nitrification, hypothesis refuted (`nitrif_cold_2026_10_22.py`)
Hypothesis: the nitrification law (fitted on warm-season corn) is too low in cold months, which would explain the engine's wheat nitrification (45% of Cycles' at N=0) and corn's low off-season nitrification (38 vs 50 kg N/ha at N=150). Test: the engine law on Cycles' own daily states (start-of-day layer-1 NH4 mass, layer-1 nitrate mass, the day's soil temperature and saturation), summed by month; corn 24 sites, wheat 8 sites, N=0 and 150. First attempt gave 1.5 to 3x because it used profile NH4; the fit used layer-1 NH4 (corrected, checked against `nitrif_timing_2026_10_20.py`).
Result: law / Cycles by month, corn N=0 0.81 to 0.97 (January to March 0.94 to 0.97, June to August 0.81 to 0.84), corn N=150 0.79 to 1.01, wheat N=0 0.70 to 0.80 in every month, wheat N=150 0.78 to 1.03. No cold-season deficit; a flat 0.8 for wheat at N=0. The law is not the gap.
So the engine's low nitrification comes from its states: ammonium is 30% below Cycles' (season mean NH4 2.0 vs 3.0 at N=0, 19 vs 28 at N=150). Attribution: setting the ammonium weight in plant uptake to zero (`NH4_UPTAKE_W=0`, nitrate only; default 1 keeps the pro rata draw, no source fixes either) lifts engine nitrification 18 to 22 (N=0) and 131 to 140 (N=150) against Cycles' 28 and 158, NH4 2.0 to 2.3 and 19.0 to 20.0: about 40% of the gap, so not adopted. Remaining suspect: the engine applies only NET mineralization to ammonium (positive to NH4, negative drawn from NH4 first), while Cycles carries gross mineralization into NH4 and immobilization out of NH4 and nitrate as separate per-layer flows (`nh4_immobil`, `no3_immobil`, `mineral` in the Cycles-L coupling, Kemanian 2024 Sec 2.6 "assimilates N from the ammonium and nitrate pools"). The pro rata or NH4-first split of immobilization is not documented. Next step if pursued: carry gross mineralization and immobilization separately (the six-pool step already computes both) and compare NH4 and nitrification levels for the two splits against Cycles before any yield test.

## 2026-10-22: gross mineralization and pro rata immobilization (`IMMOB_SPLIT=prorata`), NOT adopted
Change tested: gross mineralization goes to ammonium and immobilization is drawn from ammonium and nitrate in proportion to the two pools (Kemanian 2024 Sec 2.6 says the microbial pool "assimilates N from the ammonium and nitrate pools"; the split is not documented; the Cycles-L coupling carries `nh4_immobil` and `no3_immobil` separately). The default NH4-first form is mathematically the net form. 24-site corn budget: in-season nitrate 21.6 to 16.9 at N=0 (Cycles 16.0) and 230 to 184 at N=150 (199), ammonium 2.0 to 3.7 and 19.0 to 26.9 (Cycles profile 3.0 and 27.9), nitrification 18 to 41 and 131 to 228 (Cycles 27.9 and 157.5, overshoot +45%).
Held-out (94 sites; wheat 58), paired grain MAE gain [CI] prorata vs NH4-first: corn N=0 -0.045 [-0.067,-0.027], N=150 -0.002, N=400 -0.004 [-0.007,-0.001]; silage N=0 -0.065 [-0.099,-0.035]; sorghum N=0 -0.027 [-0.045,-0.010], N=400 -0.009 [-0.016,-0.003]; wheat N=0 -0.025 [-0.044,-0.008], N=90 -0.070 [-0.095,-0.046]. Leaching falls below Cycles (0.86 / 0.91 / 0.87 corn, was 1.24 / 1.21 / 1.10), volatilization rises to 2.1 to 2.35x at N=0 (was 1.3 to 1.7x), N2O up. Grain significantly worse in all four crops: NOT ADOPTED, default stays NH4-first.
Reading: the lumped ammonium pool is treated as layer-1 ammonium by nitrification and volatilization (surface only), while Cycles holds ammonium in every layer, so a larger lumped pool inflates both fluxes; the Stage comparison of engine lumped NH4 with Cycles' profile NH4 was not like for like. Together with the NH4-uptake-weight test this is the second failure of the same kind (rule 6). The structural fix is per-layer ammonium (tried on 2026-10-21 before the lost-root fix: N=150 leaching 1.54 to 1.84x, patch saved as `patch_nh4_layered_2026_10_21.diff`); parked. The wheat nitrification gap (engine 45% of Cycles') stays open and is not a law or a cold-season problem.

## 2026-10-22: Stockle and Campbell 1989 Eq. 17 and 18 uptake against Cycles' daily uptake (`uptake_sc89_2026_10_22.py`), NEGATIVE
Source: Stockle and Campbell 1989 (`agricultural/stocklecampbell1989.pdf`, Eqs. 15 to 18): uptake per layer U = Umax x root length x layer thickness x dt x fT(soil T) x (1 - reserve/reserve_max) x Nr/(Nr + K), temperature function Campbell form with optimum 25 C, rhizosphere concentration Nr from convection plus cylindrical diffusion. Tested as written with the reserve term dropped (Cycles has none), rhizosphere = bulk solution concentration (first-order case), root length = root biomass x exponential depth weight (scale h), three fitted parameters (Umax x length scale, K, h), odd/even site hold-out, 24 sites, N=0 and 150, 223,896 site-days with soil temperature added to the dataset (`uptake_data_2026_10_22.py`).
Daily R2 on held-out sites: N-short days (factor < 0.6, 61,141 days) train -0.14, test -5.24; unstressed days (59,143) train -0.57, test -0.84; all alive days (138,630) train -0.00, test -0.03. The depth scale h went unphysical (-2.4e11 on N-short days, a degenerate fit); without the temperature function the result is no better. The engine's pool cap on the same days: test 0.04 / -1.12 / -0.08. The weekly step was interrupted by a worker restart; the earlier weekly tests (plan entry above) already showed the soil-based forms at 0.35 and below against 0.76 for the demand of Cycles' actual growth.
Conclusion: the documented Michaelis-Menten uptake mechanism does not explain Cycles' daily uptake either. This is the third documented or standard supply form to fail (CropSyst Eq. 26 shape, mass flow, Stockle and Campbell Eq. 17), after the pool cap; by rule 6 the supply-side thread is closed unless a source for Cycles' own rule is found. Godwin and Jones 1991 (the CropSyst Eq. 26 availability functions) remains untested; given the shape results, expect the same. Uptake in Cycles tracks the growth it makes at a reduced new-growth concentration below plant N status ~0.6 (plan 2026-10-22), which the engine's stress and demand structure already reproduces in total (plant N within 4 to 7%).

## 2026-10-22 ablation, batch 1 (corn, 94 sites, continuous chain; tags frAB_*)
Each switch removed or set to a neutral/documented value versus the adopted base; grain MAE gain is base minus variant (negative = the row helps similarity).
- Snow model off: neutral (gain -0.002 / +0.002 / +0.009). Row is not earning its place on corn; keep only if a cold-site test shows a gain.
- Nitrification ratio term removed: grain neutral, but volatilization falls to 0.94 / 0.52 / 0.34 of Cycles from 1.34 / 1.06 / 0.99. The term is needed for volatilization at N>0 (class C, keep).
- Rain-interception evaporation off: worse at N=0 and 150 (-0.015, -0.046), leaching worse. Keep.
- Fe temperature variant (25 / 4.0): much worse (-0.66 at N=0). The fitted decomposition temperature stays; documented parameters do not reproduce Cycles.
- N_DEMAND_SCALE 1.0: grain neutral, leaching 0.55x at N=400 (rms log 0.95). The 0.70 scale is needed for N losses, not grain.
- SOLUTE_VOL_SAT off: leaching 1.51 / 1.42 / 1.19x, rms log up to 0.62 / 0.69. Keep.
- CANOPY_FAITHFUL off: N=150 and 400 grain worse (-0.025, -0.120). Keep.
- WUE_CO2_EXP 0: neutral (probably inert at the fixed CO2 of these runs).
- cnpow: override name did not exist, no result; not run.
Cost ranking (similarity lost when removed): Fe temperature >> canopy faithful > resint > solute volume > nitrification ratio (volatilization) > N demand scale (losses). Snow and CO2 exponent: no measurable cost.

## 2026-10-22 ablation, batch 2 (corn, 94 sites)
- `SUBDAY_DENIT=False`: identical to base to every printed digit. The within-day saturation (`_SUBDAY_REL1_EFF`) is evidently None in the continuous-chain path, so the switch is inert there; the ledger row should say so (and the first fallow window uses the older law).
- `EVAP_ZONE_DEPTH_M=None`: crashed (the zone code subtracts from it); no result. Soil evaporation cannot be ablated by override. It would need a code path for the CropSyst form on the zone state, not run.
- Third run repeated the snow ablation on corn (tag mislabelled `frAB_snowwheat`); a wheat snow test needs the wheat harness and is still open.

## 2026-10-22 ablation, batch 3: decomposition temperature with the documented c (corn, 94 sites)
The batch 1 "Fe temperature" cost (-0.66 at N=0) was my guessed To of 25, not the documentation. Stockle and Campbell 1989 Eq. 18 fixes c = 4; the engine's fitted pair is To 36.098, c 2.177. With c = 4 and one fitted parameter To = 37.5 the curve reproduces the fitted one to rms 0.017 on 1 to 39 C.
Free run, c 4 / To 37.5 versus fitted pair: grain MAE gain -0.021 [-0.033,-0.010] / -0.004 [-0.015,+0.006] / -0.015 [-0.024,-0.008] at N=0/150/400; pooled r 0.957 / 0.925 / 0.966 (unchanged within 0.002); leaching, denitrification, volatilization, N2O levels and rms log within 0.03. So the second fitted parameter is worth about 0.02 Mg/ha of grain MAE and nothing else. Class C row becomes: documented form and documented c, one fitted parameter. Not adopted yet (a small grain cost for provenance); decision for Matt.

## 2026-10-22 batch 4: documented decomposition moisture response adopted (corn, 94 sites)
Cycles docs list DECOMP_HALF_RESP 0.22 and DECOMP_RESP_POWER 3.0. Hill form r^3/(r^3+0.22^3) on relative wetness (an assumption about what 'saturation' means) replaces the 9-point fitted table (`FE_MOISTURE_DOC`). Isolated against the c = 4 run: grain MAE gain +0.003 [-0.012,+0.017] / -0.004 [-0.013,+0.003] / -0.003 [-0.009,+0.002] (neutral), leaching 1.23 to 1.19x, 1.19 to 1.16x, rms log 0.45 to 0.42 at N=0, volatilization 1.34 to 1.25x. Against the old constants (c = 4 and moisture together): grain MAE +0.018 / +0.009 / +0.019 worse, pooled r within 0.002, leaching better. Both adopted by Matt's "Go" (2026-10-22) as the source-first defaults; the two constants and the table are one switch each. Silage and sorghum reruns (new versus old constants) launched, tags frN_*; corn also shares `sixpool_fe` so wheat and soybean inherit it and still need a check.

## 2026-10-22 batch 4 follow-up: silage and sorghum with the documented decomposition factors (94 sites, new versus old constants)
Silage grain MAE gain -0.031 [-0.061,-0.005] / -0.003 / -0.002 at N=0/150/400; sorghum -0.007 / -0.015 [-0.028,-0.002] / -0.023 [-0.033,-0.014]. Pooled r within 0.005 everywhere. Leaching ratio better in all six cells (silage 1.42 to 1.38, 1.64 to 1.60; sorghum 1.27 to 1.22, 1.18 to 1.13), volatilization better at N=0, denitrification 5 to 12% higher (silage N=0 2.15 to 2.33x, so the over-denitrification worsens slightly). Same pattern as corn: a 0.01 to 0.03 Mg/ha grain cost bought with documented constants. Wheat and soybean not rerun yet.

## 2026-10-22 batch 5: nitrification moisture factor as Stockle and Campbell Eq. 8 (corn, 94 sites, `NITRIF_FW_DOC`, default off)
Against the new defaults: grain neutral (gain -0.002 / -0.020 [-0.044,0.000] / -0.001), leaching better (1.19 to 1.11, 1.16 to 1.04, 1.08 to 0.99x), but volatilization overshoots (1.25 to 1.91, 1.03 to 1.52, 0.98 to 1.37x; rms log 0.46 to 0.64). Cause: Eq. 8 is linear in saturation (0.33 at 0.3, against about 1.0 for the fitted logistic), so ammonium persists in the drier part of the range and volatilizes. Cycles' nitrification moisture response is flat at moderate saturation and falls only near saturation, unlike Eq. 8. Not adopted; the logistic stays (class D, suspect, test above). A form that is flat then falling would need a source; the Cycles docs name no parameter for it.

## 2026-10-22 source check: nitrification and denitrification in the paper, SI and Cycles-L paper
Read: Kemanian et al. 2024 (`Kemanianetal2024.pdf`), `SI.pdf`, Shi et al. 2023 (Cycles-L), Cycles docs. Result: none gives a nitrification or denitrification equation. The paper's table names the maximum rates `kan` and `knd` (kg/kg/d at optimum temperature and moisture, with tillage able to raise them) and its text says ammonia oxidation by comammox is not simulated explicitly; the SI has no nitrification, denitrification or N2O section (its N content is the microbial C:N and net mineralization, Section V); Cycles-L lists the N processes only in a mass-balance equation. Cycles docs give only calibration multipliers (`NITRIF_RATE`, `POT_DENITRIF_RATE`, `DENITRIF_HALF_RATE`, `DECOMP_HALF_RESP` 0.22, `DECOMP_RESP_POWER` 3.0). So the nitrification and denitrification laws (ratio term, moisture cutoff, nitrate x respired carbon x saturation form) cannot be moved to class A from these sources; they stay class D with the Stockle and Campbell Eq. 8 test recorded. The one documented hook left is that Cycles applies a saturation response to decomposition, which the engine now uses; whether nitrification shares it is unknown and untested.

## 2026-10-22 literature tests of the nitrification and denitrification laws (Matt: "do this right")
Sources found: DAYCENT equations S1 to S17 (Parton et al. 1996, 2001; Del Grosso 2000) in the bg-2018-126 supplement (saved by the fetch tool; the saturation function S11 is garbled in the typeset text), C-Farm overview (Kemanian and Stockle: N cycling taken from existing CropSyst algorithms, which are Stockle and Campbell 1989), Stockle and Campbell 1989 Eqs 8 to 11. Tests on Cycles' own daily states, start-of-day NH4 and NO3, odd/even site hold-out (`nitrif_daycent_2026_10_22.py`, `denit_daycent_2026_10_22.py`):
- Nitrification moisture response: fitted logistic R2 0.990 (k refit), DAYCENT bell 0.74 (medium) / 0.78 (sandy), Stockle and Campbell Eq. 8 0.57. Free-form shape of Cycles' response: 1.17, 1.11, 1.05, 0.98, 0.88, 0.69, 0.42, 0.02 for saturation bins 0.2 to 1.0, near 1 - S^4. The NO3:NH4 ratio term is worth 0.35 of R2 under every moisture form; no published source found.
- Denitrification: DAYCENT min(1.15 NO3^0.57, 0.1 CO2^1.3) times a logistic in the engine's pwp-shifted saturation, CO2 as kg C/ha/d, published numbers read as kg N/ha/d: amplitude 0.74 (so the published magnitude holds within 26%), held-out daily R2 0.74 against 0.85 for the engine's fitted law on the same rows. Nitrate exponent matters little for daily R2 (0.57 to 0.8 equal) but a lot across N rates.
- Free run (corn, 94 sites, `DENIT_DAYCENT`): amplitude 0.744 gives leaching rms log 0.37 / 0.40 / 0.29 (from 0.42 / 0.47 / 0.26), grain neutral to slightly worse (gain +0.019 / -0.023 / -0.001), but denitrification 4.06 / 1.53 / 0.78x Cycles (fitted law 2.58 / 1.33 / 0.97x). Amplitude 0.40: 2.43 / 0.90 / 0.45x. Amplitude 0.25: 1.60 / 0.59 / 0.29x. One scalar cannot fit all N rates: the published nitrate exponent is too flat compared with how Cycles scales between N rates, while the engine's nitrate state at N=0 is too high (1.5x to 2x). Not adopted; switch stays off.
Next, from sources: the shape of Cycles' denitrification across N rates is the discriminating test, not daily R2; a second documented denitrification form (EPIC, CropSyst's current N model, DNDC) would be tested the same way before any more fitting. The engine's N=0 nitrate floor (uptake cap) is the shared upstream cause of high denitrification at low N under every law.

## 2026-10-22 APEX/EPIC denitrification, the published form (corn, 94 sites)
Source: Kemanian and Williams, CEAP 2011 "APEX model upgrades" (nrcs.usda.gov, fetched with curl; the umd.edu mirror of Izaurralde 2017 has a broken certificate chain and was not used). Rdn = rho dz Rmax fNO3 fresp fw with Rmax 32e-6 kg N/kg soil/d, fNO3 = NO3/(NO3+60) (mg N/kg), fresp = min(1, CO2/50) (mg C/kg soil/d), fw = 1/(1+((1-Av)/(0.90+0.1 fclay))^-60) (Av air-filled volume). Matches the structure of the Cycles docs' POT_DENITRIF_RATE and DENITRIF_HALF_RATE and was written by the Cycles developer. `denit_apex_2026_10_22.py`: layer 1 on Cycles' daily states, no free parameter, held-out R2 0.50 (fclay 0.25) to 0.53 (0.35), start-of-day moisture best (end-of-day 0.07 to 0.18); shape agrees with Cycles above saturation 0.8 and under-predicts below 0.75 (layer 2 and wetter within-day states).
Free run (`DENIT_APEX`, per layer, engine clay, `DENIT_NLAYERS` 2), versus the adopted fitted law, grain MAE gain / leach x / denit x / N2O rms log:
- as published (amp 1.0): -0.102 / -0.073 / -0.002; leach 1.47 / 1.49 / 1.37x; denit 0.65 / 0.42 / 0.40x; N2O rms log 0.16 / 0.68 / 1.09
- amp 2.0: -0.075 / -0.043 / -0.002; leach 1.37 / 1.36 / 1.22x; denit 1.14 / 0.73 / 0.69x
- amp 2.5: -0.063 [-0.106,-0.031] / -0.034 / -0.005; leach 1.33 / 1.31 / 1.16x; denit 1.36 / 0.86 / 0.81x (denit rms log 0.40 / 0.73 / 0.84); N2O 1.19 / 0.78 / 0.67x
Reading: the published law sets a denitrification level within 15 to 35% of Cycles across N rates with one scalar, where the fitted law was 2.6x at N=0. Grain and leaching get worse because the engine carries more nitrate than Cycles at low N (earlier: 1.5x to 2x), which the fitted law's over-denitrification was cancelling. That makes the nitrate pool, not the denitrification law, the mechanism to fix: with the documented law in place its excess is visible and attributable. Not adopted yet: grain MAE cost. Plan: attribute the engine nitrate pool at N=0 (mineralization, uptake cap, immobilization draw, rhizodeposit sink) under `DENIT_APEX` amp 2.5, fix what the sources support, then re-test adoption.

## 2026-10-22 attribution of the N=0 nitrate pool under the published APEX denitrification (24 sites, `nbe_apex`, `NB_OVR`)
In-season profile nitrate, Cycles | engine (kg N/ha): April 24.7 | 38.9, May 23.2 | 32.1, June 14.0 | 22.7, July 10.2 | 16.0, August 9.0 | 12.8, September 11.3 | 14.0, October 15.1 | 16.6. Engine in-season net mineralization and nitrification are LOWER than Cycles' (April 1.3 vs 3.6, June 4.8 vs 7.5 kg N/ha per month), so the surplus is not made in the season: the engine arrives at planting 58% high and the gap narrows as the crop and the published denitrification draw it down. October agrees (16.6 vs 15.1), so about 22 kg N/ha enters between harvest and planting in the engine against about 10 in Cycles, i.e. the off-season chain (consistent with the 2026-10-21 finding that the surplus is inherited). History recorded only in-season, so winter months are not in this table. Next from science: the off-season carbon and residue turnover (Kemanian SI Eqs SI.10 to SI.14, residue decay with temperature and moisture from the documented factors now in place), tested for net mineralization timing against Cycles and, as the independent test, against measured winter nitrate dynamics.

## 2026-10-23 Mead (AmeriFlux US-Ne1, Ne2, Ne3) observation set and the weather check
Data in `agricultural/data/ameriflux/`: daily reductions of the BASE products (2001 to 2025, hourly records, so daily rain = mean x 24; with 48 the annual totals are impossible) and BIF summaries (`US-Ne*_bif.json`, from `tools/bif_extract.py`): soil texture and depth, planting, fertilizer, irrigation events (GRP_DM_WATER, amounts in the comment text), tillage, measured grain and total production (2001 to 2005, gC m-2), above-ground biomass and LAI (2001 to 2007). Sequence: Ne1 continuous maize irrigated; Ne2 and Ne3 maize in odd years, soybean in even years (Ne2 irrigated, Ne3 rainfed). Documented management (planting, N, irrigation) covers 2014 to 2021 only; LAI, biomass and yield cover 2001 to 2007 only; the engine's weather tile ends in 2016, so the overlap of documented management with engine weather is 2014 to 2016.
Weather check before any scoring (`mead_weather_check_2026_10_23.py`, 2001 to 2016, engine gridded cell against each tower): rain annual tower 851 / 856 / 701 mm against engine 799 / 794 / 793 (towers a few hundred metres apart differ by 150 mm), daily r 0.51 / 0.52 / 0.61; solar bias 0.00 / -0.12 / +0.17 MJ/m2/d, daily r 0.89; mean temperature engine +1.9 / +1.9 / +1.7 C warmer than the tower mean (part is the (tx+tn)/2 convention), daily r 0.98. Reading: radiation and temperature inputs are close; rain is the weak input (daily r about 0.5), so single-day and single-year comparisons will be noisy and the scorer should judge seasonal totals and multi-year behaviour. Next: scorer for ET (latent heat), soil water, GPP, LAI, biomass and yield, engine and Cycles on the same inputs, no engine changes.

## 2026-10-23 Mead step 1, Ne3 (rainfed maize-soybean), engine and Cycles v1.5.20 against the tower and BIF (`mead_score_2026_10_23.py report Ne3`)
Inputs identical for both models: BIF soil (35% clay, 8% sand, 57% silt, 1.8 m, organic C 3.25 / 2.80 percent in 0 to 15 and 15 to 30 cm, deeper carbon assumed 0.55 / 0.25 / 0.10 of the 15 to 30 cm value), the engine's gridded weather cell, maize in odd years and soybean in even years from 1997, planting at the documented day (2014 to 2016) else day 121 (maize) or 134 (soybean), N 150 kg/ha at planting for maize (documented 134 kg in 2015; assumption elsewhere), spin-up 1997 to 2000, scored 2001 to 2016. The engine chain needed an optional per-year plan (crop, planting day, kwargs; soybean years pass N and carbon state through unchanged); defaults are unchanged (snapshot IDENTICAL, parity WORST 0.0). History output gained soil, residue evaporation, transpiration and ETo.
Results (tower ET = latent heat / 2.45 MJ/kg; observed yields from gC m-2 at 44 percent C):
- Reference ET is the same in both models (e.g. 4.81 vs 4.80 mm/day at day 120 to 139), so differences are in the use of it.
- Seasonal mean ET, tower 3.32, engine 3.67, Cycles 3.82 mm/day; interannual r with the tower 0.60 (engine) and 0.72 (Cycles); engine against Cycles 0.95. Soil water within-season anomalies: tower~engine 0.67, tower~Cycles 0.77.
- Seasonal cycle (maize years, mm/day, tower | engine | Cycles): day 120 to 139 1.26 | 3.26 | 2.48; 140 to 159 2.06 | 4.05 | 3.49; 160 to 179 3.26 | 5.18 | 5.86; 180 to 199 4.69 | 2.86 | 4.64; 200 to 219 4.48 | 2.92 | 3.54. Both models evaporate far too much in spring (engine soil evaporation 3.4 to 3.7 mm/day, 70 to 80 percent of ETo; Cycles 2.4 to 2.9; tower total ET only 27 percent of ETo) and peak a month early; the engine's July to September transpiration sits flat at 2.6 to 2.8 mm/day against Cycles' 3.2 to 4.0 and the tower's total 3.9 to 4.7. Daily 7-day ET correlation with the tower is -0.02 (engine) and 0.29 (Cycles).
- Drought: 2012 soybean season ET tower 418 mm, engine 212, Cycles 264 (rain inputs agree within 10 mm that year). Grain yield, observed | engine | Cycles (Mg/ha): 2001 maize 7.61 | 6.89 | 8.31; 2002 soy 3.48 | 2.45 | 2.40; 2003 maize 6.75 | 3.06 | 4.28; 2004 soy 3.57 | 2.76 | 3.13; 2005 maize 7.73 | 5.64 | 7.38.
- LAI (60 dates, models via interception, k 0.55 assumed): r obs~engine 0.63, obs~Cycles 0.77; peak obs 4.5, engine 5.3, Cycles 5.5.
Reading: the engine is below Cycles in maize drought years and in July transpiration (consistent with the standing finding that its water stress is too strong), and both models share a spring soil-evaporation excess that the tower shows is real. Observation-based next steps: the documented FAO-56 two-stage soil evaporation (REW/TEW) against the tower in spring, and the engine's mid-season transpiration cap; then the irrigated sites (auto-irrigation rule in both models, documented events for 2014 to 2016 as a check).

## 2026-10-23 Mead steps 2 and 3 (spring evaporation, mid-season transpiration, irrigated sites, weather forcing)
Item 1, spring soil evaporation (Ne3): the FAO-56 two-stage Kr form (re-implemented as `SOIL_EVAP_DOC="fao56"`, Allen et al. 1998 Eqs 73 to 77; the docstring of `soil_evaporation` described it but the code had been removed) and the CropSyst Evaporator form (`"cropsyst"`) leave the spring excess unchanged: ET at days 120 to 139, tower 1.3 mm/day, default 3.3, FAO-56 3.4, CropSyst 3.1 (Cycles 2.5). Replacing gridded rain by the tower's rain does not change it either (3.3) and lowers yields. Tower energy budget in early May (maize years): Rn 127, LE 44, H 60, G 11 W/m2 (Bowen ratio 1.4); the models' 3.3 mm/day is about 116 W/m2, nearly all of Rn - G. Negative result for the evaporation law; defaults unchanged (snapshot IDENTICAL).
Item 2, mid-season transpiration (Ne3 maize, days 180 to 199): canopy 0.90 vs 0.92 and potential transpiration 6.67 vs 6.90 mm/day agree between engine and Cycles; the water stress factor is 0.46 vs 0.67 (1 = none). The engine's profile is drier in every layer (relative available water at days 140 to 159: layers 2 and 3 0.42 and 0.55 vs Cycles 0.91; subsoil 0.28 and 0.12 vs 0.56 and 0.37) and enters the season with 79 mm above wilting point against 147 mm. The loss is between January 1 and planting (engine +0 mm, Cycles +25 to +100 mm): over those 128 days the engine's soil evaporation is 152 mm against Cycles' 112 (+ residue evaporation 17.5 vs 6.7); precipitation and runoff are similar. Cause of the evaporation excess: residue cover 0.22 in the engine against 0.53 in Cycles (after maize 0.26 vs 0.42; after soybean 0.13 vs 0.58). The after-soybean figure is mostly my rotation shortcut: the engine's soybean path has no residue or carbon feed, so soybean straw never enters the pool (a known limitation of the soybean path; rotation results after soybean are not trustworthy for water and N). Soybean canopy in the engine is a few weeks behind Cycles' (interception 0.47 vs 0.75 at days 180 to 199).
Item 3, irrigated sites: the auto-irrigation rule (50 percent depletion, refill 1.0 m, days 160 to 240) applies 150 to 930 mm per season, against documented events of 206 / 193 / 125 mm (Ne1) and 190 / 160 / 71 mm (Ne2) in 2014 to 2016, so documented events are used in those years (engine `irrigation_events`, Cycles FIXED_IRRIGATION) and only 2014 to 2016 are scored for ET at the irrigated sites. Even then the models run 30 to 60 percent above the tower. Cause, found from the tower: in days 180 to 219 the gridded weather has mean temperature +3 C (27.4 vs 24.2 to 24.6), relative humidity 13 to 16 points lower (60 vs 73 to 76 percent) and wind 45 to 60 percent higher (3.9 vs 2.4 to 2.7 m/s), so its FAO-56 reference ET is 6.9 to 7.2 mm/day against 4.6 to 4.9 from the tower's own T, RH, Rn and G (3.5 against 4.8 in May). Same bias in both models; part of it is the regional grid not seeing the cooler, moister air inside a transpiring crop.
With tower-adjusted weather (gridded diurnal ranges, tower daily mean T and RH, tower wind or 0.6 to 0.7 of the gridded wind, gridded solar and rain; `wxvar="toweradj"`): mean season ET, tower | engine | Cycles: Ne3 3.28 | 3.71 | 3.68; Ne1 2014 to 2016 3.80 | 4.52 | 4.24; Ne2 3.78 | 4.26 | 3.82 mm/day (before: Ne3 3.32 | 3.67 | 3.82, Ne1 3.81 | 5.87 | 5.66 on auto irrigation, Ne2 3.85 | 5.09 | 4.78). 7-day ET correlation with the tower, engine | Cycles: Ne3 0.11 | 0.54, Ne1 0.64 | 0.71, Ne2 0.43 | 0.75. Ne3 grain yield 2001 to 2005, observed | engine | Cycles: 7.6 | 8.7 | 9.7, 3.5 | 3.5 | 3.2, 6.8 | 4.2 | 6.3, 3.6 | 3.3 | 4.5, 7.7 | 7.6 | 9.7 (gridded weather: engine 6.9, 2.5, 3.1, 2.8, 5.6). Ne2 irrigated maize and soybean yields agree for both models (12.3 | 12.6 vs 11.8 observed in 2001); Ne1 above-ground biomass 23.8 | 24.0 vs 22.9 observed in 2001, the models flat at 23.7 to 24.9 over 2001 to 2005 against an observed decline to 19.4 to 20.6.
Reading: on the tower's own air temperature and humidity the engine is within 12 to 19 percent of season ET and close on most yields, Cycles slightly closer. The engine's open Mead gaps are its day-to-day ET dynamics and July transpiration (water stress too strong, drier profile at planting from low residue cover and winter evaporation), the soybean path's missing residue and carbon feed, and a spring evaporation that both models overstate. The gridded weather's summer warm, dry, windy bias is a property of the forcing and should not be read as model error. Open: a tower-complete weather file (Tmax, Tmin, humidity range, wind) would replace the hybrid; irrigation for 2001 to 2013 is unknown so those years are not scored for ET at Ne1 and Ne2.

## 2026-10-23 verification before adding a soybean residue and carbon feed: not done, and a correction
Proposed after the Mead results, then checked against payoff. (1) Neither `engine_api.py` nor `simulator.html` has a rotation option and the done-when criteria involve none, so the fix would only serve the Mead test rotation. (2) Correction to the entry above: the engine's profile water at planting is 163 mm on tower-adjusted weather against Cycles' 147 mm; the 79 mm shown on gridded weather came mostly from the warm, dry, windy forcing, not from residue cover. (3) Sensitivity at Ne3 (tower-adjusted weather), raising engine residue cover to Cycles' levels by year type (+0.45 in maize years, +0.16 in soybean years): 7-day ET correlation with the tower 0.11 to 0.46, but planting-day water 263 mm (Cycles 147), maize grain 9.6 and 10.0 Mg/ha in 2001 and 2005 against 7.6 and 7.7 observed (2003 improves, 4.2 to 7.1 against 6.8). Cover is a sensitive lever whose correction does not line up with the observations, the compensating-errors pattern recorded in CLAUDE.md. Decision: no soybean carbon feed; rotation results after soybean stay untrusted for water and N and are not scored as a model verdict. Next candidates need a source and a measurable payoff first.

Change from baseline, engine | Cycles (year-to-year r of the change). Fitted law (today's defaults): irrigation grain +2.52 | +2.15 (0.89), denit +2.13 | -2.66 (wrong sign), leaching +0.84 | -0.27 (wrong sign); manure grain +5.04 | +4.85 (0.86), denit +21.1 | +11.3, n2o +2.94 | +1.50; late planting grain -0.34 | -0.20 (0.39), denit -0.55 | +0.10; tillage grain -0.04 | -0.39 (0.35), denit -0.52 | +5.26 (wrong sign), n2o -0.13 | +0.51. APEX law (`DENIT_APEX`, amp 2.5): irrigation grain +2.54, denit +1.43 (still wrong sign), leaching +1.05 (wrong sign); manure grain +5.22, denit +13.8, n2o +2.02; late planting grain -0.36, denit -0.07, n2o -0.07; tillage grain -0.14, denit +2.12 (right sign), n2o +0.09. Baseline level engine | Cycles: denitrification 13.2 | 15.4 (was 17.8), N2O 2.3 | 2.9 (was 3.1), leaching 8.9 | 6.1 (was 7.8). Grain responses are unchanged by the law; denitrification and N2O responses improve in three of four treatments; irrigation sign and the leaching baseline stay open.
