## 2026-10-23 ADOPTED: EPIC/APEX denitrification (`DENIT_APEX=True`, amplitude 2.5) as the default (Matt: "adopt it if the checks pass")
Rule 10 checks, each against today's defaults (fitted law), 94 sites for corn, silage and sorghum, 60 for wheat (FALLN=60): pooled r corn 0.957 / 0.924 / 0.965 to 0.953 / 0.922 / 0.965 (N=0/150/400), silage 0.933 / 0.921 / 0.954 to 0.932 / 0.920 / 0.954, sorghum 0.939 / 0.886 / 0.925 to 0.939 / 0.884 / 0.925, wheat 0.925 / 0.845 / 0.859 to 0.925 / 0.849 / 0.860 (N=0/90/150). All above 0.8. Grain MAE cost (positive = worse): corn 0.063 / 0.034 / 0.001, silage 0.043 / -0.001 / 0.000, sorghum 0.032 / 0.037 / 0.003, wheat 0.015 / 0.010 / -0.005 Mg/ha; the corn N=0 cost is above the 0.05 tolerance by 0.013 and was accepted knowingly as part of the package (the fitted law's 2.6x over-denitrification was cancelling the engine's nitrate surplus). Denitrification and N2O at N=0 improve sharply (corn denit 2.58x to 1.36x of Cycles; silage rms log 0.70 to 0.39 and 0.32 to 0.15; sorghum 0.75 to 0.41 and 0.38 to 0.18); management responses of denitrification and N2O move to the right sign in tillage, late planting and manure. Costs recorded: sorghum denitrification rms log at N=150 and 400 0.56 to 0.85 and 0.59 to 0.95, sorghum N=150 leaching level 1.13x to 1.36x, corn baseline leaching 7.8 to 8.9 kg N/ha against Cycles 6.1, irrigation response of denitrification still the wrong sign. The amplitude 2.5 is a labelled suspect (class C). Checks after the switch: `snapshot_2026_10_20.py` differs from the previous reference in 31 cases (all N-model cases; expected), new reference `/tmp/w/snap_apex_adopted.json`, `check_parity_2026_10_19.py` WORST 0.0, ledger coverage clean. `DENIT_APEX=False` restores the fitted law. The public older pages carry the 2026-10-19 engine and are unaffected. Next: the nitrate surplus upstream (off-season mineralization timing) and the irrigation sign, now visible without the compensating denitrification.

## 2026-10-23 forced mineral-N pool and the "over-denitrification" question (`npool_force_losses_2026_10_23.py`, 24 sites, corn, N=0 and 150, years 1983 on)
Cycles' daily profile NO3 and NH4 forced into the engine in every window (`E.NPOOL_FORCE`), annual means kg N/ha, engine | Cycles. N=0 denitrification: free APEX 2.5 0.7 | 0.6; forced pool with APEX 2.5 1.0 | 0.6, APEX 1.0 0.4 | 0.6, old fitted law 1.4 | 0.6 (2.45x). N=150: free APEX 2.5 13.3 | 15.4; forced pool APEX 2.5 13.0, APEX 1.0 5.4, old fitted law 17.3 | 15.4. Leaching with forced pool 1.3 | 2.0 (N=0) and 4.1 | 6.0 (N=150), volatilization 5.8 | 3.3 and 32.7 | 15.5. Findings: (1) the old fitted law over-denitrifies at N=0 even with Cycles' nitrate, so its excess was intrinsic, not a pure compensation for the surplus; (2) the gap is about 0.8 kg N/ha per year in absolute terms and the ratio is inflated by the metric; (3) the engine's denitrification rises about 13x from N=0 to N=150 under either law with Cycles' pool, Cycles' about 26x, so no single amplitude fits both rates; APEX and DAYCENT give flatter nitrate dependence, not steeper; (4) the forced-pool test is confounded by the lumped ammonium pool (volatilization 2x, leaching 0.65x), so it does not establish that off-season mineralization is the cause of the nitrate surplus; the earlier "next: off-season mineralization" is withdrawn as a stated next step. Decision: no refit of the old law; N-loss absolute errors at N up to 150 are about 1 to 3 kg N/ha per year.

## 2026-10-23 whole-engine scorecard at the current defaults (`scorecard_now_2026_10_23.py`) and a bound on the water-stress lever
94 fresh sites (spring crops, YEAR_STEP=2, continuous chain) and 60 wheat sites (FALLN=60), pooled r | bias | grain MAE (Mg/ha) at N=0/150/400 (wheat 0/90/150): corn 0.953 / 0.922 / 0.965 | +10% / +2% / -6% | 0.51 / 0.94 / 0.85; silage 0.932 / 0.920 / 0.954 | +8% / -3% / -7% | 0.94 / 1.64 / 1.61; sorghum 0.939 / 0.884 / 0.925 | +3% / -3% / -11% | 0.50 / 1.12 / 1.16; soybean 0.943 | -8% | 0.42 (no N model, same at every N); wheat 0.925 / 0.849 / 0.860 | +4% / +8% / -1% | 0.41 / 0.78 / 0.76. N losses, engine/Cycles kg N/ha per year (corn): leaching 6.1/4.6, 17.4/13.3, 98.8/84.8; denitrification 3.5/2.6, 15.7/18.3, 53.6/65.8; volatilization 3.5/2.7, 15.2/14.4, 39.1/39.6; N2O 0.6/0.5, 2.8/3.6, 10.5/15.7. Silage and sorghum are the same pattern (leaching 1.3x to 1.6x at N up to 150, denitrification and N2O 0.7x to 0.8x at N=400). Soybean has no N-loss output at all (Cycles: leaching 7.0, denitrification 6.2, volatilization 4.5, N2O 1.0 kg N/ha per year) and the wheat harness records none. All five crops pass criterion 1 at every N rate (lowest: wheat N=90 0.849, sorghum N=150 0.884).
Bound on the water-stress lever (24 sites, corn, Cycles' own daily water-stress series forced into the engine, `corn_n0_attrib_2026_10_17.py` MODES=free,water): grain MAE 0.375 to 0.404 (+7.7 percent worse) at N=0 and 0.960 to 1.062 (+10.6 percent worse) at N=150; bias +0.07 to +0.17 and -0.12 to +0.42; r 0.81 to 0.79 and 0.74 to 0.64. Against Cycles the engine's water stress is balanced by other terms (compensating errors), so a water-stress change alone is negative; the Mead drought-year shortfall (five yield-years at one site) is not enough evidence to take it on. Not pursued.

## 2026-10-23 provenance sweep: which fitted (D) and assumed (E) ledger rows have a source in hand
Cheap checks done (no engine change): (1) Residue cover: Gregory (1982) Fc = 1 - exp(-Am M) with Am about 0.40 ha/Mg for maize and 0.45 to 0.54 for wheat (French review table and a wheat quotation; soybean not found) against the engine's fitted 0.27 per Mg/ha of AG+BG residue; not a free reclassification, stays D until the difference (pool definition or weighting) is explained. (2) N demand scale 0.70: the crop file carries `N_MAX_CONCENTRATION` (0.055 g/g) and `N_DILUTION_SLOPE` (0.4) plus grain and straw min and max and `N_PARTITIONING_FACTOR`; the docs define the curve but give no reason for a 0.68 to 0.75 factor, so it stays D. (3) Cold damage: Cycles docs give the exact rule (daily minimum below `MIN_TEMPERATURE_FOR_COLD_DAMAGE` multiplies existing above-ground biomass and interception by 1 - 0.99^3 = 0.03 and sends the killed biomass to residue; phenology delayed; with `FLOWERING_TT` passed the crop is harvested), the engine ends the season at one threshold; documented, convertible E to A. (4) N2O from denitrification: APEX Eq. 5, frN2O = fNO3 (1 - fw^0.5)(1 - fr^0.33), uses the same factors as the adopted denitrification; convertible D to A; N2O from nitrification still has no APEX term. Order of the sweep and the named source hunts are in the message of this date; each conversion is adopted under rule 10.

## 2026-10-23 sweep step 1, cold damage per the Cycles docs: NOT implemented (negative result)
Docs: graded daily damage factor f = 1 - (T - Tmin)/(Tthresh - Tmin) (capped at 0.99), above-ground biomass and interception multiplied by 1 - f^3, killed biomass to residue, phenology delayed, harvest trigger after flowering. Crop file values: corn and silage -5 / 3 C, soybean -5 / 2, sorghum -1 / 6, winter wheat -25 / -10. The engine implements only the harvest trigger and an opt-in soil-temperature winter kill. Exposure of the documented rule (cumulative from planting, which overstated wheat because winter wheat at northern sites does not emerge before winter): spring crops 20 percent of site-years with any damaging night before flowering, mean cumulative multiplier 0.98, none below 0.5. Test against Cycles v1.5.20 daily output (`dailyWinterWheat.csv`, `dailyCornRM.90.csv`, northern sites f006, f011, f024, f054, f067): 58 nights with Tmin below -10 C and live above-ground biomass of at least 0.02 Mg/ha, none with a biomass drop above 0.5 percent although the formula on the weather-file minimum predicts 10 to 40 percent; corn, 427 nights where the formula predicts more than a 10 percent loss, median Cycles biomass ratio 1.000 (for example 1983-09-23, Tmin -0.8 C, documented x0.89, Cycles x1.003). The documented rule is not visible in Cycles v1.5.20 output with the weather-file minimum, so implementing it would add damage Cycles does not show. Cause not identified (candidates: a canopy or surface temperature instead of air minimum). Decision: no change; ledger row updated with the test. Cost of the step: one session of probes, no engine change.

## 2026-10-23 N2O share of denitrification from APEX Eq. 5 (provenance sweep step 2)

Check first: Cycles' own N2O_from_denitrification / denitrification (dailyN.csv, 24 sites, N=150, 149k days with denitrification above 1e-3) against fNO3 (1 - fw^0.5)(1 - fresp^0.33) built from Cycles' start-of-day states. Mean 0.081 vs 0.079, daily corr 0.73, nitrate-binned levels agree; the one structural gap is near saturation (Cycles about 0.02, Eq. 5 about 0). Implemented as `N2O_APEX` (default True). Engine, 94 fresh sites, continuous chain: grain MAE, bias and pooled r identical (diagnostic only). N2O vs Cycles improves at N=0 (level 1.19 to 0.84, rms log 0.17 to 0.14) and worsens at N=150 and 400 (0.78 to 0.55 and 0.67 to 0.47 of Cycles, rms log 0.45 to 0.58 and 0.65 to 0.90), because engine denitrification is already 0.86x and 0.81x of Cycles' there. The cost sits in the denitrification amplitude suspect (2.5) and moisture state, not in the partition. Snapshot differs only in `n2o`; parity WORST 0.0.

## 2026-10-23 Residue cover constant explained (provenance sweep step 3)

Sources checked: Gregory 1982 (ASAE, Fc = 1 - exp(-Am M); maize about 0.40, wheat 0.45 to 0.54 per Mg/ha via a French review table and a Purdue/ISCO summary), Cycles docs (STANDING_RESIDUE_AT_HARVEST: standing residue is in minimal contact), Cycles output (dailyResidue radiation_interception; standing and flat residue carbon in dailySoilLayersCN). Cycles' interception = 1 - exp(-0.384 M_flat - 0.084 M_standing), rms 0.003, against 0.0185 for one K on total mass (K 0.272). So the fitted 0.27 is a blend of a Gregory-sized flat coefficient and a small standing coefficient, not an error. Bound on building the split: cover error is at most 0.03 (May to August), about 2 to 3 percent of ETo in soil evaporation, so not built. No engine change; ledger row reclassified (cover C, rain interception D).

## 2026-10-23 Source hunts for fitted rows (provenance sweep step 4)

N uptake supply limit: retested with the current engine, cap removed on 94 sites: N=0 grain MAE 0.50 to 0.95 (bias +0.19 to +0.76), N=150 0.95 to 1.20, N=400 unchanged; leaching level 1.33 to 0.88 and 1.31 to 1.18, rms log 0.47 to 0.55 and 0.57 to 0.48. Still fails; thread stays closed (plan 2026-10-22). Decomposition rate constants: no public source for Cycles' reference rates (paper table has no values, Pravia 2019 paywalled, Clivot 2019 is another model). Volatilization: temperature slope plausible against thermodynamics (recalled coefficients, unverified); wind and moisture terms unsourced. No engine change. Remaining ledger D rows have a named owner of the missing source: Pravia 2019 tables, Godwin and Jones 1991, or the Cycles developers.

## 2026-10-23 Pravia 2019 decomposition constants adopted as a package; Godwin and Jones 1991 read

Sources now in the repo (on `main`): Pravia et al. 2019 and Godwin and Jones 1991 (chapter 13 of Hanks and Ritchie, Modeling Plant and Soil Systems). Pravia Eqs 1 to 4: Cx = 0.021 + 0.038 fclay (same as the engine), ks = fe ft kx fs, kx = 0.00015 d-1, fs = 1 - 1/(1 + ((Cs/Cx)/0.22)^3). The engine carried 0.000208, (4.26, 2.53) and a fitted depth decay 0.108. Swapped as a package (rule 10), 94 sites corn, silage, sorghum and 60 wheat sites: pooled r unchanged or up at every N rate, grain MAE costs 0.00 to 0.056 (silage N=150 0.056, wheat N=0 0.037 and N=150 0.026, wheat N=90 gain 0.043), leaching and denitrification level closer to Cycles for every crop, N=0 bias moves from about +0.2 to about -0.2 (corn, sorghum, silage +0.26 to -0.31). Adopted. Silage N=150 bias is now -0.53 (N stress calibration still open). Godwin and Jones Eqs 19 to 28: demand = deficiency demand + new growth at critical concentration; supply per layer = RLV x SMDFR^2 x DLAYR x 100 x (1 - exp(-0.0275 NO3 ppm)) x 0.006 kg N/ha per cm root; uptake = min(demand, supply). Not tested against Cycles: it needs root length density, which neither Cycles output nor the engine carries (the engine has root mass).

## 2026-10-23 silage N=150 bias: attribution, and the measured evaporation law retested after the Pravia change (NEGATIVE)

Attribution (24 sites, silage forage, forced Cycles states, `attrib_ag_2026_10_18.py`): N=150 free engine/Cycles 0.927 (MAE 1.83); forcing N stress 0.911, canopy 0.941 (MAE -38%), water 1.030 (MAE -9%, corr falls), all three 0.977 (MAE -50%). N=0: free 0.763, all three 0.953. So the bias is not N stress. Free canopy against Cycles by thermal-time fraction (silage, N=150): ttf 0.2 to 0.4 engine 17 to 20% low, 0.5 to 0.6 level, 0.7 to 0.9 4 to 5% high. Forcing Cycles' water factor closes the early gap (ratios 1.05, 1.02 at ttf 0.2 to 0.4, then 9 to 10% high); forcing N stress does not move it. Cause: the engine's early-season water factor, the same open item as corn (plan 2026-10-18: layers 1 and 2 0.012 to 0.020 m3/m3 drier than Cycles', Campbell ratio 0.89 against 0.98 at emergence); silage shows it more (17 to 20% against corn 8 to 10%).
Retest of the evaporation law measured on Cycles (`EVAPP=0.25,0.85,3.5`) against the current defaults (Pravia constants), 94 sites. Bias at N=0/150/400: corn -0.18/-0.02/-0.49 to +0.15/+0.40/-0.03, silage -0.31/-0.53/-0.94 to +0.49/+0.56/+0.22, sorghum -0.27/-0.33/-0.86 to +0.03/+0.08/-0.42. Grain MAE: corn 0.472/0.955/0.850 to 0.432/1.042/0.928 (N=150 and 400 worse, CIs exclude zero), silage 0.879/1.696/1.611 to 0.906/1.678/1.507 (CIs include zero), sorghum 0.507/1.097/1.161 to 0.450/1.126/1.174 (N=0 better). Pooled r down 0.01 to 0.02 at N=150 and 400 for all three (corn 0.921/0.965 to 0.911/0.956, silage 0.920/0.954 to 0.906/0.946, sorghum 0.887/0.925 to 0.868/0.902); leaching worse at N=0 (corn 1.08 to 1.25, silage 1.14 to 1.42). Not adopted. Reading: with the evaporation law the high-N under-yield disappears and the engine is left with a response to N that is too steep between 0 and 150 and too flat from 150 to 400 (corn +0.15/+0.40/-0.03); the two errors (water too stressed, N response too peaked) cancel at the current defaults, so neither can be fixed alone (same pattern as 2026-10-19).

## 2026-10-23 package test: measured evaporation law with an N response correction (NEGATIVE, no correction exists to apply)

Cheapest test first (rule 5): corn, 24 sites, `EVAPP=0.25,0.85,3.5`, Cycles' canopy (C), water (W) and N stress (N) forced one at a time (`attrib_ag_2026_10_18.py`). Engine/Cycles yield free and MAE change from forcing: N=0 0.999, N forced 1.144 (MAE worse 32%), C 0.905 (better 16%), CWN 0.918 (better 49%); N=150 1.019, N 1.012 (better 8%), C 0.989 (better 24%), W 1.042 (worse 10%), CWN 0.974 (better 41%); N=400 0.988, N 0.991 (2%), C 0.973 (22%), CWN 0.975 (31%). Reading: with the evaporation law the free run on this 24-site set is level at every N rate (94-site bias +0.15/+0.40/-0.03), forcing Cycles' N stress moves N=150 yield by 1%, so the N stress law is not where the too-steep 0 to 150 response comes from, and the package hypothesis (an N response correction) has no lever. At N=0 the engine's N stress is stronger than Cycles' (forcing Cycles' raises yield 14%), the old early-season N=0 finding. The largest remaining lever at every N rate is the canopy (16 to 24%), then water. No change made; evaporation law stays off.

## 2026-10-23 step 5 scoping: corn-soybean rotation, Cycles against the engine (`rotation_scope_2026_10_23.py`, `rotation_engine_2026_10_23.py`, 24 sites, 1983 to 2016, corn N=0 or 150, no N on soybean)

Cycles' own rotation: corn after soybean 3.09 / 7.66 Mg/ha at N=0 / 150 against continuous corn 1.43 / 7.02 (ratio 2.16 / 1.09); soybean 3.08 / 3.14 Mg/ha with fixation 157 / 129 kg N/ha and 111 / 113 kg N/ha removed in grain. Corn-year N losses in rotation against continuous: leaching 2.8 / 4.9 vs 2.0 / 5.8, denitrification 2.7 / 13.0 vs 0.6 / 15.2 kg N/ha; soybean-year leaching 2.5 / 4.5, denitrification 2.4 / 4.8.
Engine (year_plan rotation, soybean path passes mineral N and carbon through unchanged): corn after soybean 1.48 / 6.66 Mg/ha, ratio to Cycles 0.48 / 0.87, MAE 1.65 / 1.37, corr 0.86 / 0.90; soybean 2.97 / 3.13 (correct). So the whole rotation benefit (1.6 Mg/ha at N=0, 1.0 at N=150) is missing, and the soybean year contributes no losses or soil N dynamics.
Documented mechanism (Kemanian 2024 Sec 2.5): legume N demand uses the same dilution-curve new-growth demand as other crops, soil supplies first, "N demand above that supplied by the soil is met through N fixation without luxury consumption". Engine today exempts legumes from the whole N path (`n_tracking_active = not legume and ...`), so soybean neither draws soil N, nor returns residue N, nor runs the six-pool through its season. Smallest faithful build: let the legume run the existing N path with the stress forced to 1 and fixation = unmet demand, record fixation, return residue and root N at harvest. Fixation total and residue N return to be checked against Cycles' annualN fixation and dailyResidue.

## 2026-10-23 legume N path built (step 5), corn-soybean rotation against Cycles

Change (`cycles_engine_validate.py`, crop key `legume_n`, default off so every existing result is identical: snapshot IDENTICAL, parity WORST 0.0): the legume runs the existing N path with the N stress forced to 1 and the simultaneous solve skipped; the soil supplies first (same supply cap as corn), fixation = unmet new-growth demand (paper Sec 2.5), recorded as `n_fixed_kg_ha` and `n_plant_kg_ha`; at harvest the above-ground residue N = plant N x (1 - grain share 0.54 - root share 0.065), measured on Cycles' harvest.csv (857 soybean crop-years: grain N / plant N 0.54, root 0.065, above-ground residue 0.39; plant N 203 kg/ha, grain N 39 kg per Mg, fixation 142 mean, soil uptake about 55 to 61). Soybean config `engine_api._SOYBEAN_N` adds n_demand_scale 0.74 (0.70 left plant N at 192 against Cycles' 203; 0.74 gives 203 / 208).
Results (`rotation_engine_2026_10_23.py`, 24 sites tuned on, then 24 untouched fresh sites, 1983 to 2016, corn N=0 / 150, none on soybean). Corn after soybean engine / Cycles: before 1.48 / 3.09 = 0.48 and 6.66 / 7.66 = 0.87; after 2.98 / 3.09 = 0.96 and 7.10 / 7.66 = 0.93 (MAE 0.67 / 1.05, corr 0.90 / 0.91); fresh 24 sites 3.28 / 3.30 = 0.99 and 7.54 / 7.93 = 0.95 (MAE 0.57 / 0.93, corr 0.958 / 0.949). Soybean yield 3.04 / 3.11 vs Cycles 3.08 / 3.14 (unchanged); fixation 151 / 124 kg N/ha vs 156 / 129; plant N 203 / 208 vs 203. Losses by phase (tuned sites): corn year leaching 4.3 / 7.4 vs Cycles 2.7 / 4.9 (engine high, as in continuous corn), denitrification 2.2 / 9.2 vs 2.7 / 13.1; soybean year leaching 3.6 / 6.0 vs 2.4 / 4.5, denitrification 2.1 / 4.7 vs 2.4 / 4.7. The soybean path now has N loss output. Not done: wheat in rotation, N losses for the continuous soybean harness and the wheat harness, simulator exposure of rotations, fixation timing against Cycles' daily output.

## 2026-10-23 rotations in the simulator

`engine_api.py`: crop `rotation` (corn in the first year and every second year after, soybean between, both on the corn planting window), soybean from `_SOYBEAN_N`; fertilizer, manure, tillage, irrigation apply (N and manure to corn years only); cover crops refused; extra annual columns Crop and N fixed by soybean; `simulator.html` shows string cells and excludes the Crop column from the means. `check_parity_2026_10_19.py` gained a rotation block (API against the harness chain with the same year plan): WORST 0.0. Real Pyodide 0.26.2 (headless Chromium, npm runtime): all six crop options run, rotation 5.5 s for 38 years. Per-site mean soybean fixation, engine against Cycles: corr 0.94 / 0.95 at N=0 / 150 on the tuned 24 sites, 0.97 / 0.97 on the untouched 24; engine and Cycles both show near-zero fixation at nitrogen-rich sites (Central Iowa preset: soil supplies the whole demand, `n_fixed` 0). Annual table means mix corn and soybean years (noted on the page). Not done: starting the rotation with soybean (the engine chain crashes in a soybean first year; the first year is always corn), a rotation with wheat, a gap-year design.

## 2026-10-23 scorecard at current defaults and observation checks

Scorecard (`scorecard_now_2026_10_23.py`, runs `cur_*`: 94 fresh sites, YEAR_STEP=2, continuous chain, no overrides; wheat 60 sites FALLN=60). Pooled r | bias | grain MAE (Mg/ha) at N=0/150/400 (wheat 0/90/150): corn 0.959 / 0.921 / 0.965 | -9% / 0% / -6% | 0.47 / 0.95 / 0.85; silage 0.937 / 0.920 / 0.954 | -9% / -5% / -7% | 0.88 / 1.70 / 1.61; sorghum 0.945 / 0.887 / 0.925 | -14% / -5% / -11% | 0.51 / 1.09 / 1.16; soybean 0.943 | -8% | 0.42; wheat 0.920 / 0.847 / 0.858 | -16% / +4% / -3% | 0.45 / 0.74 / 0.79. All pass criterion 1. Losses engine/Cycles, kg N/ha per year (N=0/150/400): corn leaching 4.9/4.6, 15.3/13.3, 93.1/84.8; denitrification 2.6/2.6, 13.8/18.3, 50.2/65.8; volatilization 3.0/2.7, 14.2/14.4, 38.1/39.6; N2O 0.3/0.5, 1.7/3.6, 7.0/15.7. Silage leaching 5.2/4.6, 16.7/11.7, 99.3/85.1; sorghum 5.0/4.3, 16.0/13.3, 96.1/86.1. Reading: leaching absolute error at N<=150 is +0.3 and +2.0 kg N/ha for corn (+5.0 silage at N=150), inside the 1 to 3 kg target for corn and sorghum; N2O is about half of Cycles' at N>=150 and denitrification 0.7x to 0.75x there (known cost of the APEX adoption, plan 2026-10-23); the continuous soybean harness still records no N losses (engine 0, Cycles 7.0 leaching); the wheat harness records none. Nothing regressed against the earlier figures (corn r 0.959/0.921/0.965 unchanged).
County yields (`county_runs_2026_10_20.py`, `county_compare_2026_10_20.py`, NASS 1982 to 2016, 94 sites, engine rerun at current defaults): within-site detrended r engine / Cycles: corn 0.43 / 0.38 (paired +0.039 [+0.007,+0.073], 69 sites), soybean 0.34 / 0.33, silage 0.31 / 0.34, wheat 0.15 / 0.22; level (model over county, 2007 to 2016) corn x0.93 (east 0.98, west 0.64, irrigation), soybean x1.22, silage x0.97, wheat x1.52; extreme years east corn: 1988 obs -23%, engine -24%, Cycles -43%; 2012 obs -12%, engine -34%, Cycles -41%; 1993 missed by both (+2%, +7%). Unchanged from 2026-10-20 within noise.
Mead (`mead_score_2026_10_23.py report Ne1/Ne2/Ne3`, gridded weather, BIF-measured yields, only 2 to 3 corn and 2 soybean years per site have yield records): corn grain obs / engine / Cycles Mg/ha: Ne3 rainfed 7.36 / 6.39 / 6.66 (MAE 1.52 / 1.17), Ne2 irrigated 11.70 / 12.39 / 12.52 (MAE 0.70 / 0.83); soybean grain Ne3 3.52 / 2.84 / 2.76, Ne2 4.03 / 4.49 / 4.44; above-ground biomass both models 10 to 15% above observed at the irrigated sites. 2003 drought corn at Ne3: observed 6.75, engine 3.56, Cycles 4.28 (both too low, same direction as the county 2012 loss exaggeration). Season ET both models 16 to 47% above the towers (3.85 and 3.82 vs 3.32 mm/day at Ne3, about 5.0 vs 3.85 and 5.6 vs 3.8 irrigated), the known gridded-weather bias shared by both. The legume path on versus off (corn after soybean at Ne3): 2001 8.42 vs 6.89, 2003 3.56 vs 3.06, 2005 7.18 vs 5.64, observed 7.61 / 6.75 / 7.73; Cycles 8.31 / 4.28 / 7.38. So the soybean N credit moves the engine toward both Cycles and the observations. Samples are tiny (n of 2 to 5 per cell), so this is consistent evidence, not a test.
